## 38. Gauntlet: the verification program

The Gauntlet is the subsystem that decides whether a gate may record PASS. Its crate is `frob-conformance` (layer 7).
It owns the tiers G0 to G5, the oracle fixtures and their pins, the known-answer suite (Appendix M), the
counterexample catalogue and the negative-control registry seeded from it (Appendix N), the registry of metamorphic
relations, the standard battery, the chaos schedules, the pilot register, and the status file `registries/gates.toml`.
It also hosts the harness of the ten-fold test, whose rules are §39.

"A false certificate is worse than an ordinary wrong answer" (FACT: FrankenSim's agent instructions). The Gauntlet's
first duty is therefore to the things that issue certificates and verdicts: the certificate checker, the
qualification lane, the solvability oracle, the rank-agreement ledger, the monitors and the statistics. Its second
duty is to the plan's own claims: a number in this document is a TARGET or a FACT about something else until a gate
records it, and the Gauntlet is the only path by which it becomes a result.

### 38.1 What it owns, and decisions

| Owned thing | Where it lives | Consumers |
|---|---|---|
| Tier runners G0 to G5 | `frob-conformance/tests/`, one test target per tier | CI, milestone exits |
| Oracle fixtures, pins, capture scripts | `frob-conformance/fixtures/`, `tools/oracle/` | G1, G2 |
| Known-answer suite, one derivation file per row | `frob-conformance/registry/known_answers.toml`, `docs/derivations/` | G1; Appendix M is its printed form |
| Negative-control registry | `frob-conformance/registry/controls.toml` | Every gate; Appendix N is its seed |
| Metamorphic relation registry | `frob-conformance/registry/relations.toml` | G3 |
| Standard battery and its frozen identity | `frob-conformance/battery/`; identity in the ledger | Fidelity ledger, divergence ledger, detectors (§16, §23, §29) |
| Chaos schedules and minimized regressions | `frob-conformance/chaos/` | G4; §29.15 |
| Pilot register | `frob-conformance/registry/pilots.toml` | Work-package funding (§44) |
| Gate status | `registries/gates.toml` | README status table; §40 |
| Ten-fold harness | `frob-conformance/tenfold/` | §39 |

- **ADR-GAUNT-01. The Gauntlet has no agent verb and no `xtask` subcommand.** Its tiers are test targets of
  `frob-conformance`, run by `cargo test -p frob-conformance --test <tier> -- <gate or row>`. A run writes a
  hash-named receipt; the recording session enters the row of `registries/gates.toml` that cites it. Reasons: a
  harness driven through the surface it judges is a path to self-certification (§38.11), and `xtask` holds exactly
  six checks, runs no physics and records no gate (§34.6). The verbs under test are called by the harness exactly
  as an agent would call them.
- **ADR-GAUNT-02. Registries are data and the document is their printout.** Appendices M and N, the gate register of
  §40 and the README status table are generated from `registries/gates.toml` and the registry files of
  `frob-conformance`. Reason: a hand-edited table drifts from the
  tests that are supposed to back it. A KA or CX number is never reused and never renumbered; new rows append.
- **ADR-GAUNT-03. Every gate is registered with its failing control before its passing test is written.** Reason:
  "A gate no deficient model can fail validates nothing" (FACT: the suite's Wright Flyer plan). The control is the
  specification of what the gate detects.
- **ADR-GAUNT-04. Foreign tools are comparison instruments, never dependencies and never baselines of record.** They
  run out of process, under a pin, on fixtures captured by script. Reason: FrankenSim's adapter rule (FACT), and the
  finding that a sibling's training code violated three of the rules it would have been an oracle for (§33.11).
- **ADR-GAUNT-05. Ceiling first.** No speed or efficiency experiment runs until the largest ratio it could show has
  been computed from a phase breakdown. Reason: the planning pilot of §38.5 measured 1.345 on a test whose ceiling
  was about 1.46; the experiment could not have confirmed its claim, and nobody had computed that beforehand.
- **ADR-GAUNT-06. One fixture identity per physical case.** A case stated by a construction (§10) and again by the
  subsystem that implements it is one fixture with two rows: the first tests the mathematics on the host and the
  second tests the subsystem on its tiers. Reason: two copies of a fixture's constants are two chances to disagree.

Scope cap: 24 thousand lines (§34). The crate depends on everything and nothing depends on it.

### 38.2 Six tiers

The tiers are FrankenSim's (FACT), read for robots. A test belongs to exactly one tier and names its execution site:
`host` (exact or double-precision arithmetic in a test binary), `auditor` (`frob-verify`), `referee`, `strict`
(`batch-cpu` under the `strict` profile), `lockstep` (the throughput tiers), `cuda`, `metal`, or `daemon` (`frobd`
under the simulation runtime of §29.15).

| Tier | Kind | Content carried | Content added |
|---|---|---|---|
| G0 | Laws and self-tests | The algebraic laws of §13, functor laws of C-12, cone laws, dimension checks on every kernel | Claim-product laws (§8, §37.10); the level series α·m/(m + 1); polarity laws of C-18; certifier self-tests (§38.12); layer and dependency checks (§34) |
| G1 | Known answers | Analytic and synthetic truths of the chapters | The whole of Appendix M: 835 rows at plan time (FACT, counted from the registry's seed) |
| G2 | Parity | Agreement with pinned oracles (§38.3) and published benchmark problems | Incumbent simulators as comparison instruments; conventional solvers against family answers (§25.22); published anchors for Ground (§30.2) |
| G3 | Metamorphic | Relations that hold without knowing the answer (§38.7) | Relations generated by the new constructions: 42 further relations in §38.7 |
| G4 | Chaos | Cancellation at every boundary, injected faults, capacity overflow, device loss, malformed input | Crash consistency of the job and chunk protocols; deterministic simulation of the daemon and ledger; fuzzing of the thirteen format readers; the confirmation firewall |
| G5 | Determinism | Twice on one machine; across worker counts; debug and release; across platforms; across backends, each against its class | Batch-composition invariance; decision-level equivalence under D3; exact-integer and guarded kernels; refusal of paired statistics under an uncontrolled class; D4 results refused until re-evaluated (§35, §37.11) |

The same six tiers, read for the subsystems this version adds. Each cell names the leading test; the gates are the
owning chapter's.

| Subject | G0 laws | G1 known answers | G2 parity | G3 metamorphic | G4 chaos | G5 determinism |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Phylum (§17) | Padding is exact; map merge is commutative, associative, idempotent | Orbit counts 86,185, 500,500 and 92; bucket bound 1/(1 − f + f/r + h) | A mixed cohort against P homogeneous runs; the incumbent mixed-batch rate (0.86 of homogeneous, FACT to re-read) as context only | A world of a mixed cohort equals the same world alone; slot permutation permutes results | A variant row changed mid-step; a bucket overflow | Digests at capacities 32, 64 and 128 and in any co-tenancy |
| Augur (§25) | Proof rules sound on their side conditions; partition coverage | The stance, pusher, docking, pad, jaw and label fixtures | A lifted robust linear program, a scenario tree and quantifier elimination return the same family answer | Cell refinement only resolves cells; revalidation polarity | A budget stop returns complete cells and the unresolved remainder (§25.3.4) | Identical bundle bytes on a second machine |
| Nemesis (§26) | Bounded weights at most 1/ε; zero-failure arithmetic | Planted traps, the random-walk level probability, the Gaussian tail | Card-uniform randomization at equal budget as the comparison arm | A stored `.fmcw` replays to its state hash; removing any edit of a 1-minimal world removes the failure | A fork with no fresh randomness is refused | Replay bit for bit on the tier that found the failure |
| Autopsy (§27) | Shapley values sum to v(N) − v(∅) | Planted causes; the thermal re-run; first divergence at tick 1 | Exact Shapley over 1,024 coalitions against the budgeted summary | The null fork is the original; a placebo flips nothing on a non-chaotic case | A checkpoint with its packet queue stripped is refused | Attribution in double precision; `precision-sensitive` label |
| Ground (§30) | Conformal index arithmetic; 19/21 coverage | The synthetic rehearsal (planted clock, aliases, omitted law) | Published anchors, as Parity only | Re-running the replication kit reproduces the frozen prediction | Read before freeze, refit without a version, holdout reuse: each refused | The truth simulator is the referee under a pinned seed |
| Foundry (§31) | Dimension typing of every structural quantity | Cantilever, two-bar optimum, fatigue block, rainflow sequence, tolerance split | `fs-solid` through the airlock against the hand derivations | Rainflow in windows with carried residues equals the whole-history count | An open shell, an oversize part, a divergent loads–mass loop | STEP round trip replays bit for bit |
| Qualification (§24.13) | Σ α/[i·(i + 1)] = α·m/(m + 1) | 2,000 no-signal campaigns with 500 adaptive retries | — | The decision of request i does not depend on requests after it | 10⁴ fuzzed read attempts return no sealed field | `frob-verify` replays levels, commitments and cluster derivation |
| Clock contract (§20.4) | Integer schedule equals rational schedule for all time | L = 1,200; periods 3, 12, 40; repeat 120 | — | Re-phasing; checkpoint at any instant; independent same-instant events commute | A checkpoint just after a shutter opens, after a dropped packet, before a controller update | Packet history byte for byte across tiers |
| Transactional step (§16.5) | Sub-intervals sum to the base interval exactly | Ballistic impact time; tied sliders; noise refinement | Contact-free reference robots against the pinned double-precision engine (§38.3) | A retry equals a clean run with the same plan | 10⁵ faults between check and commit: zero uncommitted reads | Forced retry digest equals the clean digest |

### 38.3 Oracles and pins

| Oracle | Pin | Use | Posture |
|---|---|---|---|
| `fs-mbd`, `fs-contact`, `fs-query` | The FrankenSim commit in `franken-stack.lock` | Parity for batched kinematics, dynamics and primitive distances; the referee itself | Consume |
| MuJoCo C, double precision | An exact release, recorded with the fixture | Parity for contact-free dynamics of reference robots; import fixtures | Development-time, out of process |
| A reference rigid-body library with analytical derivatives (optional) | Exact release | Parity for derivative kernels | Development-time, out of process |
| FrankenTorch autograd | The pinned commit | Gradient oracle for on-device learning kernels | Oracle |
| FrankenSciPy `solve_ivp` (DOP853) | The pinned version | Reference trajectories for smooth known answers | Oracle |
| FrankenSymPy evaluation | The pinned commit | Reference values for derived arithmetic; second checker of rational and interval certificates, never linked into the auditor (PO-LEMMA-04) | Oracle |
| FrankenNumPy `fnp-linalg` | The pinned commit | Double-precision oracle for small-matrix kernels | Oracle |
| Analytic derivations | `docs/derivations/`, one file per known answer | Known answers | — |
| Incumbent batched simulators (the stacks of §1 and §36: a CPU engine, its GPU ports, and the learning stacks built on them) | Exact releases and container digests chosen by the rival audit (§39) and frozen in `frob-conformance/registry/comparators.toml` | Comparison instruments for throughput and training-time parity (§36); context for mixed batches (§17); the proxy-adequacy pilot (§19) | Out of process, on `trj`; never linked, never a truth |
| The development oracle | One incumbent simulator at a pinned release (OPEN-GAUNT-4) | Paper-scale pilots that must run before the CPU referee exists (§22.17, §23.22, §24.17, §26.15, §27.17) | Out of process, outside the product; every result labeled `development-oracle` and re-run on the referee when it exists |
| The incumbent harness of the ten-fold test | The frozen comparator set of §39 | The rival arms of the sealed suite, measured with the same phase ledger | Out of process; operated under the neutral adjudicator's rules (§39) |
| Conventional solvers (a linear-program solver, a satisfiability solver, quantifier elimination) | Exact releases | Parity for family answers (§25): the generic method is the comparator a mission query must not lose to | Out of process |
| `fs-solid` and `fs-topopt`, through the airlock | The FrankenSim commit in `franken-stack.lock` | Parity for the Foundry fixtures against their hand derivations (§31.4) | Consume |

Rules:

1. A fixture is captured by a script under `tools/oracle/`, stored with its pin and date, and **never regenerated
   from the candidate** (INV-GAUNT-01).
2. Moving a pin is a deliberate event with its own record. A comparator pin does not move between the freeze and the
   end of the study that uses it (§39).
3. Foreign tools run out of process and never enter a production dependency graph (FrankenSim's adapter rule, FACT;
   checked by the dependency firewall of §33.7).
4. **Agreement with an oracle is Parity. It is never reported as Reality** (INV-GAUNT-03). This holds for every row
   above, including a published figure for the same robot (`published-anchor`, §30.2) and including an incumbent
   that has itself been compared with hardware: its agreement with reality is its authors' claim under their
   conditions, and agreement with it transfers none of that.
5. An oracle is admitted like a dependency: a pin, an admission test, a negative control. A sibling routine is not
   an oracle because it exists. FACT to recheck (§33.11): the suite's PPO update fails three of the rules of §23.6 and
   `planar_drive` returns zero translation on a near-straight case; both are held out of every oracle role until
   their admission tests pass.
6. Two oracles that disagree are a finding, filed with both pins. The candidate is not tuned toward either until
   the disagreement has an explanation in a derivation file.
7. A result obtained on the development oracle funds or kills a work package and supports no gate. When the same
   pilot is re-run on the CPU referee and the verdict differs, the referee's verdict stands and the difference is
   recorded against KILL-DEVORACLE.

### 38.4 The form of a method experiment

A method experiment asks whether a mechanism of this plan (a ladder rung, a family policy, an adversarial search, a
shield, a kernel specialization) does better than doing without it. Every such experiment is registered in the
ledger before it runs and has six parts. An experiment that lacks one is not admitted, and a result from it cannot
be cited by a gate (GATE-GAUNT-09).

| Part | What is written down before the first run | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Ceiling | The largest effect the experiment could show, computed from a phase breakdown measured on the baseline arm | A test whose ceiling is below its success threshold cannot confirm anything (ADR-GAUNT-05) |
| Competing arms | The mechanism; the strongest conventional alternative at equal resources; and the mechanism's own fallback. Where an incumbent could host the same method, a composed-incumbent arm (§39) | Every planning pilot's strong baseline tied or won (§38.5); an arm against a weak baseline measures the baseline |
| Potency control | A planted effect of the declared size that the experiment must detect, and an A/A arm that it must not | An experiment that cannot see a planted effect is silent about a real one; an A/A campaign that clears 1.02 has a broken harness (§36.16) |
| Paired lower bound | Arms are paired by world address and seed; the reported quantity is a lower confidence bound on the paired ratio or difference, never a ratio of means. Ten pairs of constant ratio r must return the bound r | Pairing removes world variance; a bound, not a point estimate, is what a kill rule reads |
| Pre-declared fallback | What ships if the falsifier holds, named with its kill gate | The decision is made before the data can argue |
| Reachability pre-analysis | For a fixed-n study: every verdict the rule can return at the planned n and level, and the power at declared alternatives (`eval.preanalyze`, §24.12). For an anytime-valid study: the budget at which the e-process can cross its threshold under the declared alternative | At 64 pairs the frozen rule of fixture 1 cannot return one of its four verdicts (FACT, §24.10); a verdict that cannot be reached must not be promised |

Three further rules. The two study types of §24.12 are never mixed inside one experiment. Paired statistics are
refused under an uncontrolled determinism class (§35). A confirmatory experiment about a design or a policy runs in
the qualification lane (§24.13) at the level of its request; the development batch that suggested it keeps its
original analysis and is not counted again.

A worked reading of the table, on the plan's own draft: a decision rule "the point estimate is at least 10 on every
brief" confirms a true ratio of 10 in at most 18% of simulated campaigns (FACT, planning pilot; Appendix K). The
rule was replaced, before any measurement, by a pooled ratio with a paired lower bound and a power analysis (§39).

### 38.5 Paper-scale pilots before funding

Every Frontier and Moonshot mechanism has a pilot: a small experiment with existing tools, outside the workspace,
that runs before the mechanism's work package is funded (§44). A pilot record in
`frob-conformance/registry/pilots.toml` states the mechanism and tier, the protocol frozen before execution, the
falsifier, the computed ceiling of the benefit, the fallback and kill gate, the cost, the tool and its pin, and
then the result. The pilots still to run are tabulated in
the owning chapters (§10 per construction; §19.10, §19.12, §20, §21, §22.17, §23.22, §24.17, §25.27, §26.15, §27.17,
§30.22, §31.19); the Gauntlet owns the register and the rule, not the protocols. A work package whose pilot's
falsifier holds is not funded; its fallback ships (GATE-GAUNT-08).

**Pilots already run at plan time.** Each row is FACT about a small program on a small model (planning reproduction
bundle; Appendix H gives its location and digest, Appendix K the review record). Each is re-run at M0 inside this
repository and recorded before any gate cites it.

| Pilot | What it measured | Result | Consequence in this plan |
|---|---|---|---|
| Static stance family | Two exact classifiers of body–load feasibility; a projection against a lifted robust program | 11,520 of 11,520 states agree; 2,254 feasible allocations; 114 of 2,304 bodies cover the mission; 102 of 102 conventional-solver spot checks agree; both programs return half-width 3/14 m in one solve each; a support-polygon-plus-capacity screen wrongly accepts 1,596 bodies | The family query is sound; it has no speed advantage over a standard lifted program |
| Stance falsifier | The same body under equal passive springs | (175, 115, 85, 25) N against a 160 N rating; the active allocation peaks at 155 N | The authority model is part of the claim |
| Material probe | A generated probe, a programmed expert and direct measurement over 900 cases and four laws | 675 of 675 in class and 0 of 225 on the withheld law; expert equals generator; direct measurement 900 of 900 | Probe verdicts are conditional; direct measurement is always admitted |
| Pad family | Minimum certified mass for a 75 N pull | 300 g and 240 g at centred nominal readings only; an admitted reading drops the bound to 73.79 N | Certificates are bound to their reading set |
| Sensor aliasing | Exact Bayes success with repeated binary reads | 27/32, 459/512, 3807/4096; a shared error bit stays at 3/4; a stop-at-gap-two rule reaches 9/10 in 16/5 expected reads | Error dependence and deadlines decide sensor budgets |
| Docking, two gains | Exact three-tick docking with one velocity packet | ε < (U + 1/2)/4; of 561 cells, 344 admitted, 217 excluded, 9 on the boundary; all 561 late-packet cells excluded; 51 linear programs agree | Observation timing decides feasibility; a scenario tree gets the same answer |
| Docking, three gains | The gain 33/32 added to the support | Exact docking unsatisfiable; worst error at least 1/65 m; 34,590 positive labels per grid reverse | Certificates are bound to the declared hypothesis support |
| Pusher | A specialized solver against a composed one on ten fixtures; six corrupted certificates | 20 of 20 intervals match in each arm; six of six bad certificates rejected; median time ratio 1.345 warm and 1.088 cold | The speed gate failed; no new kind of answer on this plant |
| Pusher audit | The timing ceiling; quantifier elimination from the raw plant | Shared checking and serialization about 576 µs, 94% of the specialized batch, so a zero-cost solver reaches about 1.46 (stated; see Appendix M, M.38); an off-the-shelf engine answered 8 of 12 queries directly | The speed test had no power: the origin of ADR-GAUNT-05 |
| Labels and deadlines | Payload-width frontier against number and timing of labels | 3/32, 7/32, 11/32 mm for one, two, three early labels; 3/32 mm late; one label unsatisfiable, two satisfiable | Deadlines change feasibility; ordinary elimination answers the query |
| Decision reuse | A semantic receipt against a hermetic dependency graph over four edit states | Both: 3 pair evaluations and 2 reductions; every verdict matches fresh replay; omitting one dependency inherits a stale promotion | No reuse advantage over a correctly keyed graph |
| Shared bus | Two fingers on one unit-current bus | Zero one-tick schedules; two two-tick schedules; an additive peak rule falsely prunes | A negative control for contract compilers |
| Fixed-n rule | Enumeration of the frozen rule | At n = 64: 2,145 count pairs, 521 prefer, 521 inferior, 0 ties, 1,103 inconclusive; a tie first reachable at n = 86 | Reachability pre-analysis is mandatory (§24.12) |
| Draft ten-fold rule | Simulated campaigns under a true ratio of 10 | The draft rule confirms in at most 18% of campaigns | The rule of §39 was rewritten |
| Boundary screen | A one-inequality classifier on 65,536 rows on the M5 Ultra and on each RTX 4090 | Bare single precision: 452 false admissions and 23,438 false exclusions, identically on every device; guarded with exact fallback: zero final errors and identical bytes; exact integer kernels: zero errors | Strict predicates need exact or guarded kernels (§35) |
| Static against dynamic sill | Sampled statics against a planar dynamic scorer, 156 cases | 94 agree (7 pass both, 87 fail both); 62 static rejections succeed dynamically; an audit found scorer defects | A static rejection is a candidate for a dynamic test |
| Wheel-loop feedforward | The winding term of a sampled wheel controller | One-tick spectral radius 1.4820 with R·i_ref and 0.9207 with R·i; 24 of 156 task labels change | The hold is modelled exactly (A-22) |
| Rotor inertia and cadence | An exploratory planar sweep on the sill fixture | Success fractions 0.093 and 0.780 at 100 Hz with zero rotor inertia; 1.000 and 1.000 at 1 kHz or with added inertia | A comparison pins rotor inertia and controller rate |
| Half-precision conditioning | Contact states of three robots after unit-diagonal scaling | κ·u ≥ 1 in 37 to 64% of states; refinement fails outright in 9 to 18% | Tensor-core half precision is rejected (§21.20) |
| Humanoid conditioning | 201 poses with simplified contact rows | κ₂(H) of 3·10⁴ to 1·10⁵ raw and 2·10³ to 9·10³ scaled at default impedance; 2·10⁶ to 3·10⁶ raw and 2·10⁵ to 5·10⁵ scaled near-rigid | Guarded single precision with scaling (§35.10) |

What these pilots did not do is part of the record.

- **None used FrankenSim dynamics, FrankenSim contact or a real sensor pipeline.** The analytic pilots used small CPU
  programs and conventional solvers; the native runs executed one scalar decision kernel; the planar sill pilots
  used their own law and scorer. No hypothesis of this plan was tested by running FrankenRobotics code, because
  none exists.
- **Every pilot's strong baseline tied or won.** A lifted linear program tied the projection; a programmed expert
  tied the probe generator and direct measurement beat both on the withheld law; exact Bayes tied the sensor
  calculation; a scenario tree tied the docking family; a dependency graph tied the receipt; quantifier elimination
  produced the pusher answers. What survives is an interface and a discipline, not a speed claim.
- **No physical claim follows.** The material laws are artificial, the loads stipulated, the plants scalar.

### 38.6 Known answers

Appendix M is the suite: 835 rows numbered KA-001 onward, grouped by chapter, each with its truth, tolerance, tier
and negative control (FACT, counted at plan time; the registry is the authority and the appendix its printout). It
replaces the list of version 0.2 (group and cone laws; mobility of Sarrus, Bennett and four-bar linkages; growth
vectors; Elroy's beanie; pendulum, top and ballistic invariants; the contact battery; motor and thermal constants;
sensor readings; the mass-spiral fixed point; leg inverse-kinematics solution counts; ledger closure for a damped
pendulum), every item of which is carried as rows.

Rules for a row:

1. **A derivation on file.** `docs/derivations/<fixture>.md` states the model, the derivation, the numeric value with
   its digits, and the error budget of the test (truncation order, round-off bound, tier envelope).
2. **A tolerance with a reason.** `exact` for integers, rationals, Booleans, verdicts and digests. For real values,
   the tolerance of the owning gate, scaled as §35 requires. A row printed with tolerance `to set` is not eligible to
   pass until its derivation file states one (OPEN-GAUNT-2; 133 rows at plan time).
3. **A negative control.** The mutation or planted failure that must make the row fail. A row printed with
   `none stated` cannot support a gate until a control is registered (OPEN-GAUNT-3; 325 rows at plan time). A
   boundary behaviour ("a 4 mm film is refused") is not a control; the registry needs a mutation.
4. **A tier and a site.** A row runs first on the weakest site that can decide it, then on every tier its gate names.
   A truth that holds on the referee and inside the fidelity envelope on the fast tiers is two results, not one.
5. **Values are not improved in transit.** The suite carries each chapter's printed value. Where a recomputation
   disagrees, the row keeps the printed value, is listed in Appendix M (M.38), and its gate cites the derivation file
   once OPEN-GAUNT-6 is closed.

### 38.7 Metamorphic relations

Generated from Part II (D-4) and from the constructions. Each relation is a test over random scenarios on every tier
it names, registered with the mutation that must break it. The first table is carried.

| Relation | Source |
|---|---|
| Applying a symmetry element to the initial state and the actions gives the symmetry element applied to the result | C-09 |
| Changing the world frame by a horizontal translation and a yaw changes nothing an invariant observation reports | C-09 |
| Re-expressing every quantity in another body frame leaves powers and energies unchanged | C-02 |
| Scaling the unit system leaves dimensionless outputs unchanged | C-14 |
| Halving the time step moves results by the integrator's order | C-05 |
| Permuting world indices permutes the results and changes nothing else | §16 |
| Adding inactive worlds or unused capacity changes no active world's digest | §16 |
| Refining the scene field moves contact results by no more than the interpolation bound | §19 |
| Splitting a rollout at a snapshot and resuming equals running it whole | §29.8 |
| An edit followed by its inverse returns the same `DesignId` | §14.7 |
| A design built with `mirror` equals its hand-expanded form | C-12 |

Relations added by the constructions of this version.

| # | Relation | Source | Mutation that must break it |
|---|---|---|---|
| MR-01 | **Batch-composition invariance.** A world's digests are identical alone, in any batch, slot, co-tenancy, bucket capacity (32, 64, 128) and shard count of one device family | INV-KIN-04, INV-NUM-03, GATE-KIN-05 | A reduction tree shaped by capacity; a batch-wide normalizer |
| MR-02 | P parameter variants in one batch equal P homogeneous runs bit for bit; a world of a mixed cohort equals the same world in a homogeneous batch | GATE-KIN-06, INV-PHYLUM-02 | A parameter row read after its first use in the step |
| MR-03 | A draw depends on (address, step, stream) only; adding a stream changes no other stream | INV-NUM-04, §35.9 | Sequential draws from one generator |
| MR-04 | **Orbit representatives.** Evaluating any member of an orbit under the observation-preserving subgroup and transporting the result equals evaluating the representative; g·d in world w behaves as d in g⁻¹·w | C-22, §17.11 | Deduplication under the mechanical group when a sensor breaks it |
| MR-05 | Designs with equal mission-observable projections replay bit-identically; two placements with equal first moment and different second moment do not merge | C-22, §28.15 | A digest that omits the second moment |
| MR-06 | **Similarity rescaling.** Every dimensional input scaled by its dimension with power-of-two factors (lengths × 4, times × 2, masses × 2, gravity unchanged) reproduces every significand bit and branch | C-21 | A scale factor of 10 |
| MR-07 | The Cholesky factor of D·H·D equals D times the factor of H bit for bit, for power-of-two D | PO-NUM-02, PO-TRAC-03 | A scaling by 10 instead of a power of two |
| MR-08 | **Fork and replay.** The empty intervention replays bit for bit; a setpoint change at tick t reuses the prefix to the snapshot before t; a child with its own address has fresh streams | C-27, C-29, §29.8 | A checkpoint with its packet queue stripped; a fork that shares streams |
| MR-09 | A retry of a transactional step equals a clean run with the same plan; the noise path is unchanged under split, rejoin, reject, checkpoint restart and device migration | INV-KIN-02, INV-KIN-03 | Redraw on rejection (P(positive) = ¾) |
| MR-10 | **Revalidation after an edit and its inverse.** An edit followed by its inverse restores every verdict with zero evaluations; an edit to a field the mission does not read keeps every result | C-18, §28.13, GATE-PHYLUM-05 | A memo key without delivery time |
| MR-11 | Polarity: an inclusion survives the world set shrinking, the policy class growing and assumptions weakening; an exclusion survives the three opposite moves | C-18, PO-LEMMA-03 | Hash match taken as verdict transfer |
| MR-12 | Incremental recomputation equals full recomputation on random edit sequences | INV-SKEL-02, INT-FSIM-16 | An input missing from a key |
| MR-13 | **Qualification under adaptive retries.** On no-signal worlds the campaign-wide false-promotion rate stays at most α for any number of retries and any adaptive strategy; the decision of request i is unchanged by later requests | §24.13, GATE-QUAL-01 | Reused clusters at a constant level |
| MR-14 | An anytime-valid study's level holds at every stopping time; a fixed-n study's verdict is computed once | C-15, §24.12 | Peeking: 13/256 after 8 looks |
| MR-15 | **Clock re-phasing.** Nominal event k is at phase + k·period whatever the physics step; the integer schedule equals the rational schedule for all time; schedules are identical on every tier | PO-SENS-01, INV-SENS-04 | Every-thirteenth-step scheduling of a 30 Hz policy (30.77 Hz) |
| MR-16 | Checkpoint and restore at any instant, including just after a shutter opens, after a dropped packet and just before a controller update, reproduces every later delivered observation byte for byte | INV-SENS-03, §11.7 | Packet queues left out of the checkpoint |
| MR-17 | Swapping independent events that share an instant leaves digests unchanged | §20.4.7, C-19 | An ordering by arrival |
| MR-18 | Causality: two worlds that differ only in something not yet delivered give equal command digests; an out-of-order arrival never changes a past decision | §20.15, §11.8 | A policy that reads truth behind an undelivered sensor |
| MR-19 | **Unit normalization of requirements.** A change of units leaves normalized robustness and the binding atom unchanged | C-30, §24.5 | Composition of raw margins |
| MR-20 | Streaming past-time robustness equals offline evaluation; G over [0, T] at 0 equals H over [0, T] at T | C-30, GATE-CORTEX-10 | A monitor that pads a short window |
| MR-21 | A rate reward totals the same at any control rate; physical discounting values a constant reward rate the same to first order at any control interval | §23.3 | A per-step penalty (500 against 1,000) |
| MR-22 | The energy budget is unchanged by retraining and by any contact friction coefficient μ ≥ 0, any restitution and any contact law | C-31, GATE-SHIELD-01 | A budget with an omitted source |
| MR-23 | Re-description of a robot (relabeling, frame change, axis-sign convention, power-of-two scaling) leaves a family policy's actions unchanged after mapping back | C-23 | File-order tokens |
| MR-24 | Policy transport: a zero-parameter edit gives a child whose first action equals the parent's; commuting edits give the same initialization in either order | C-23 | Transport by index instead of by matched token |
| MR-25 | Equivariance under the observation-preserving subgroup with noise stream identifiers permuted by g; an element outside that subgroup is not a symmetry | §20.13, C-09 | Tying under the mechanical group |
| MR-26 | A film law at zero thickness equals the dry card bit for bit and is continuous in thickness; doubling pad radius multiplies squeeze time by 16 | INV-TRAC-07, §19.10 | A masked, non-conservative film transport |
| MR-27 | The load-weighted slip metric is unchanged when a quadrature point is duplicated with halved weights | §24.8 | Unweighted point speeds |
| MR-28 | The mirrored robot on the mirrored course returns the mirrored result; forces reflect as polar vectors and moments as axial ones | §24.11, C-09 | Angular velocity treated as polar |
| MR-29 | Clearance: inner complex ⊆ free space ⊆ outer complex; a finer grid only narrows the band; both complexes shrink as the margin grows | C-33, §19.11 | A scalar clearance radius |
| MR-30 | The pooled scene field equals the dense field bit for bit; a scene regenerated from its seed has the same `SceneId` on both machines | GATE-HAB-13, INV-HAB-10 | A generator that reads an unseeded source of randomness |
| MR-31 | A mirror image, a clutter variant, a retrofit variant and another scan of the same building belong to the split group of the base scene | §19.14 | A split keyed by `SceneId` alone |
| MR-32 | Integer histograms and address-keyed trace sampling are invariant under permutation, shard split and slot assignment | §29.6, §29.7 | An order-dependent sketch; arrival-order sampling |
| MR-33 | The tier-corrected estimate has the same mean for every fast-tier prediction; with every address audited it is the plain mean | C-25, §29.11 | Audit bits chosen after the outcomes |
| MR-34 | Rainflow counted in windows with carried residues equals the whole-history count | C-35, §31.8 | `finish` called per window |
| MR-35 | Canonical bytes: key order and whitespace do not change an identity; view → canonical → view and canonical → value → canonical are exact; re-serializing a bundle gives identical bytes | §34.9, PO-LEMMA-01 | Key-order canonicalization disabled |
| MR-36 | A repeated idempotency key returns the original receipt; a page sequence pinned to a frontier shows each row once under concurrent appends | §32.7, §32.8 | Two-statement lookup and insert; offset pagination |
| MR-37 | A job run through the worker process returns the digests of the same job run in process, also after the worker is killed and the job resubmitted | INT-FSIM-18 | A worker built at a different commit |
| MR-38 | A class-E rewrite changes no bit; a specialized kernel is bit-identical to its generic kernel | §21.7, INV-SPARK-09 | A reassociation filed as class E |
| MR-39 | A ranking survives the model-form variants (CL-1 against CL-2, cone against pyramid, stiffness × ½ and × 2, h against h/2) with top-k overlap at least k − 1, or is typed `model-conditional` | §16.9 | A finalist that wins by diagonal traction on a pyramidal cone |
| MR-40 | The claim product is order-independent and never raises a class | §8, INV-LEMMA-02 | A function that returns a stronger class than its inputs |
| MR-41 | Holonomy: a shape loop doubled doubles the displacement, reversed negates it, and sped up leaves it unchanged, to leading order | C-08 | The curvature computed with +[𝔸ᵢ, 𝔸ⱼ] |
| MR-42 | A cell shift of an anchored position is exact in either direction (cell side 8 m, offset in [−4, 4) m) | §11.1 | An offset range of [0, 8) |

### 38.8 The standard battery and the hard-world set

**The standard battery.** For each robot class, about 200 scenarios (TARGET): stand, push recovery, each atlas gait
at three speeds, turning, stairs up and down, a slope, a low-friction patch, a payload, and a drop, crossed with
randomized parameters under fixed seeds. Wheeled, tracked and wheel-legged classes replace the gait rows with
straight travel, turning in place, a sill, a ramp and a split-grip strip. It is defined and frozen at M2 (BD-104).
The fidelity ledger, the divergence ledger, the tier-correction ledger and the exploitation detectors all read it,
so a change to the battery is a recorded event with a new battery identity, and a silently shortened battery
changes identity (GATE-ARENA-07). The batteries `smoke`, `fixtures`, `full`, `hard` and `covering` are Arena's
(§24.9); `standard` is the one this chapter owns, and it is the fallback of the covering selection (§26.11).

**The hard-world set.** For each task family and robot class, Nemesis keeps a versioned list of minimal
counterexample worlds (`frob.hardset/1`, §26.8). The Gauntlet reads it under three constraints, each enforced by a
refusal that exists already: it is discovery data and never a confirmation sample (FROB-REF-567); it is never the
nominal draw of a rare-event bound (FROB-REF-560); and it is never the only content of a battery, since a battery
made of past failures overfits them. A world enters only with a `solvable-causal` or `plant-feasible` witness and
after promotion on the referee. Regression use is legitimate: a design that once failed a hard world is re-run on it
after a repair, and the result is labeled as a regression result, not as evidence of robustness.

**The fixtures.** `fixture:cart-sill-dock` and `fixture:quad-split-grip` (§24.10, §24.11) are the two full fixtures
with an analytic layer, a frozen development batch and a pre-analysis. Their analytic layers run on every change
that touches contact, actuators, clocks or the requirement language.

**The design corpus.** About 200 designs with planted defects of known kind, planted winners for the screening
audit and planted single causes for attribution (§24.9). Labels are fixed before any analysis is tuned. The corpus
is the potency control of §38.4 for every analysis and screen.

### 38.9 Chaos

Every case below must end in its typed refusal or in a state the invariants accept, with no leaked resource.

| Family | Injection | Oracle | Owner of the mechanism |
|---|---|---|---|
| Cancellation | At every kernel-batch boundary under a deterministic schedule; at every await point of the publish and submit paths | The receipt reports the last committed tick per world; a dropped handle is not a cancellation receipt | §21, §32.10 |
| Device faults | Device loss mid-step; a kernel cache entry with one flipped byte; an arena lease revoked; a strict-backend mismatch before scoring; out-of-memory at admission | A chunk replayed from the committed slab gives the unfaulted digests; the flipped entry is refused | §21 |
| Transaction faults | 10⁵ faults between the checks and the commit of a transactional step (TARGET) | Zero uncommitted reads: every read names a `commit_epoch` | §16.5 |
| Job protocol | A crash after the idempotency commit and before the receipt; duplicate and reordered delivery; a client that disconnects with a job running and reconnects by submission key; two requests with one key interleaved | One job, one charge, the original receipt | §32.7, §32.10 |
| Chunk protocol | A torn ledger write; a crash after commit; lease expiry and takeover by a second writer; overlapping tick ranges after a shard split; a reset that reuses tick zero; a full disk; a clock jump | One row and one receipt per extent; different bytes for a committed extent are FROB-REF-810; the frontier is contiguous | §29.4, §32.10 |
| Daemon and ledger | Deterministic simulation with virtual time, seeded scheduling, lineage-driven fault selection and schedule minimization (FACT: asupersync's lab runtime); a fake device whose outcomes are a pure function of the world address | INV-CHRON-01 to -04 and -12, INV-ATEL-02 and -06, one job one charge; dual runs against `batch-cpu` keep the fake honest | §29.15 |
| Capacity | Contact rows, islands, packet queues and buckets forced past capacity | The typed refusal or the `capacity-biased` label, never a silent truncation | §16, §17 |
| Malformed input | Fuzzing of the thirteen format readers, the envelope parser and every importer (10⁷ executions each, TARGET); path traversal; hostile strings in imported text | No panic; each limit reached by a crafted file and refused; no hostile string outside an untrusted-marked field | §34.9, §37 |
| Confirmation firewall | 10⁴ fuzzed read attempts against a sealed campaign; a planted leak path | No sealed field returned; the leak found by the audit | §24.13, §37.5 |
| Worker fallback | The FrankenSim worker killed mid-job and resubmitted | Bit-equal chunk digests | §33.14 |

TARGET: 10⁵ seeded schedules per release, every await point on the publish and submit paths hit by a crash fault at
least once, and every failing schedule minimized to a regression that replays in under a second (§29.15).

### 38.10 The Reality lane

Reality is the one claim the first release mostly cannot make. Version 0.2 kept a lane here so that the first
admitted dataset had somewhere to land. That lane is now Ground (§30), which owns the admission of data, the cell
that scopes a result, clock fitting, identifiability, frozen predictions on sealed trials, conformal bands per
calibrated cell, drift retirement and the replication kit. The Gauntlet keeps four duties toward it.

1. **The line.** Every report prints `reality: <status> […]`, and with no dataset the status is `no-claim`
   (GATE-GROUND-01). `no-claim` is a legitimate terminal state. FrankenTextiles recorded the same limit about its own
   domain: "More numerical permutations cannot supply those observations" (FACT).
2. **The label.** A comparison with a published figure for the same robot is scheduled by the Gauntlet at M3 and is
   recorded as Parity with status `published-anchor`, never as Reality (rule 4 of §38.3). A comparison with data
   from a simulator standing in for reality is `synthetic` and is never promoted (INV-GROUND-06).
3. **The rehearsal.** Ground's machinery is tested without real data on the synthetic rehearsal of §30.21, whose
   rows are in Appendix M. Passing it shows a bounded loop is sound and nothing more.
4. **The share of guesses.** A report that supports a benchmark claim prints the share of card fields whose
   provenance is `generic` or `assumed` (§30.2). Agreement between tiers cannot reveal a wrong card, because every
   tier consumes the same card.

No exported artifact carries `sim_to_real_certified = true`; the flag has no setter (§12.2, §37.9).

### 38.11 Guarding against false progress

The sibling projects documented how agent swarms produce the appearance of progress. These rules are the response.

- **Typed close reasons.** A bead closes with a reason that cites its own test and that test's output. A shared
  close reason across many beads is itself a failure signal.
- **Negative controls.** Every gate has at least one planted failure it must catch, registered in
  `frob-conformance`. "A gate no deficient model can fail validates nothing" (FACT: Wright Flyer plan).
- **Live-dependency proofs.** For a gate that claims a result depends on an input (a scene, a card, a policy), the
  test perturbs the input and requires the output to change.
- **Synthetic is labeled.** Output computed from a fixture or a stand-in prints `synthetic` in its evidence.
- **Behaviour, not plumbing.** A learning gate needs a learned behaviour and failing controls: zero-weight, random
  and shuffled-label policies must fail the same threshold (§23.21).
- **No self-certification.** A gate's PASS is recorded by a fresh-context session that did not implement the
  capability.

**The negative-control registry.** `frob-conformance/registry/controls.toml` holds one record per control:

```toml
[[control]]
id        = "NC-0412"                       # stable, never reused
seed      = "CX-262"                        # the catalogue entry it came from, if any
gate      = ["GATE-ARENA-04"]               # every gate that must catch it
kind      = "mutation"                      # mutation | planted-input | planted-design | planted-world | a-a
target    = "frob-actuate::stage::integrate"
defect    = "explicit Euler, current before wheel speed, at 0.5 ms"
expect    = "wheel-loop row reports spectral radius 1.497 where 0.9207 is true; the row fails"
last_seen = { caught = true, session = "…", date = "…" }
```

The registry is seeded from Appendix N: each of its 384 entries (FACT, counted at plan time) becomes at least one
control, attached to the gates of the rule it protects, and each `Negative control` cell of Appendix M becomes one.
Five rules govern it.

1. A gate with no control in the registry cannot record PASS (INV-GAUNT-02, GATE-GAUNT-01).
2. A control is run every time its gate is run. A gate run in which a control was not caught is a FAIL of the gate,
   whatever the passing test says.
3. A control is a defect, not a boundary. "The request is refused at the limit" tests the limit; the control is the
   build or input in which the limit was removed.
4. A control that has never failed its gate in any recorded run is itself suspect and is reviewed at each milestone
   exit: either the defect cannot be planted as described or the gate is not looking.
5. A false certificate, a false promotion or a withdrawn result found in use becomes a permanent control with the
   counterexample that exposed it (§25.5.5).

### 38.12 Certifying the certifiers

These are G0 tests of the Gauntlet itself and of everything that issues a verdict. Carried: the certificate
verifier must reject mutated certificates; the dimension checker must reject seeded violations; the e-process must
hold its error rate under the null with optional stopping; the physical-consistency test must reject a matrix one
eigenvalue below zero. Added:

| Certifier | Test | Threshold (TARGET) | Owner's gate |
|---|---|---|---|
| The certificate checker (`frob-cert`, `frob-verify`) | A mutation corpus per proof kind: changed coefficient, reordered quantifiers, missing side condition, wrong frame or unit, fabricated kind with consistent hashes, a flag set to true, a boundary opened or closed; valid specimens re-serialized | Every mutant rejected and every specimen accepted, with identical bytes on re-serialization; standalone and in-daemon verdicts identical on the whole corpus; dependency count zero | GATE-LEMMA-03 to -06, GATE-WS-03 |
| The checker before an answer | `frob-verbs` runs the checker before a mission answer returns (§12, §25.5); a producer that emits a false bundle on a planted lowering defect | The answer is withheld and the cell is downgraded to `Tested` or left unresolved | §25.5 |
| The qualification lane | 2,000 no-signal campaigns with 500 adaptive retries by a scripted adversary that sees every receipt and all discovery data | False promotion in at most 123 campaigns; a planted true gain promoted within 10 requests in at least 80%; reused clusters at a constant level give a false promotion in more than 99% | GATE-QUAL-01 to -03 |
| The solvability oracle | Planted illegal edits, impossible traps and solvable traps | 100 of 100 refused; 0 of 100 impossible traps counted; at least 95 of 100 solvable traps receive a witness; a point-robot oracle is caught on the narrow doorway | GATE-NEMESIS-01 |
| The rank-agreement ledger | Planted populations with known top-weighted rank correlation on each side of each threshold in force (0.7 and 0.5, §28.10); a planted winner the cheap rung represents poorly | The measured top-band τ covers the planted value in its bootstrap interval; the mode assigned is the one the thresholds prescribe; a lost planted winner stops the rung from discarding in that stratum; an unmeasured stratum is `discard-only` | GATE-ATEL-07; KILL-RANK |
| Screening audits | A deliberately biased screen and an unbiased one | The planted winner's removal is reported; the unbiased screen passes; 30 clean audited discards bound the rate at 9.5% | §26.10, §28.12 |
| Requirement monitors | Three evaluators on 10⁶ random traces per operator | Agreement bit for bit | GATE-ARENA-03 |
| The fixed-n pre-analysis | The tables of §24.10 regenerated | Exact equality; the unreachable verdict refused | GATE-ARENA-05 |
| Rare-event bounds | The Gaussian tail 3.167 × 10⁻⁵ and the random-walk probability 1.504 × 10⁻⁴ | Truth inside the confidence sequence in at least the stated share of repetitions; every weight at most 1/ε | GATE-NEMESIS gates of §26.15 |
| Attribution | 200 planted single causes; joint and alternative pairs; a chaotic placebo | The planted factor first with `but-for` in at least 90%; `chaotic: unattributable` on the doubling map | GATE-AUTOPSY-03 |
| Conformal bands | Exchangeable residuals at n = 20, target 0.90 | Mean coverage within 0.01 of 19/21; a pooled shifted regime is flagged | GATE-GROUND gates of §30.22 |
| The claim product | Random sets of input evidence | The envelope's class equals the meet; no public function raises a class | GATE-SAFE-08 |
| The determinism classifier | A planted nondeterministic reduction; a D4 result offered unlabeled | Classified below its claimed class; refused on every path | GATE-SAFE-09; §35 |
| The energy audit | The stiff penalty bounce; a sign error at one port | `energy-created` raised; the error localized to its port | GATE-CHRON-03 |
| The Gauntlet's own harness | Ten pairs of constant ratio r; an A/A campaign | The paired lower bound equals r; A/A does not clear 1.02 | GATE-GAUNT-09 |

### 38.13 Status accounting

`registries/gates.toml` holds one record per gate, kill gate and admission gate (421 identifiers at the end of Phase
1, FACT; the register of §40 is its printout, and where a chapter and the register differ the register governs, §40).

```toml
[[gate]]
id         = "GATE-ARENA-05"
claim      = "known-answer"        # proof | known-answer | parity | fidelity | design-comparison | reality | performance
claim_id   = "clm:…"               # §34.12
section    = "24.17"
threshold  = "eval.preanalyze reproduces the tables of §24.10 exactly and refuses the unreachable verdict"
milestone  = "M1"
tier       = ["G1:host"]
controls   = ["NC-0398", "NC-0399"]
status     = "not-run"             # not-run | pass | fail | killed | no-claim | superseded
measured   = ""                    # the value with its unit and interval, or the count
evidence   = []                    # ledger sequence numbers and trace identifiers
recorded   = { date = "", session = "", implemented_by = "" }
```

Rules. `claim` takes one of the seven kinds of §8 and nothing else; a gate whose chapter row reads "Performance of
the method" is recorded as `design-comparison`, since it compares methods and does not measure the engine. `pass` is
refused when `controls` is empty, when any control was not caught in the same run, when `recorded.session` equals
`recorded.implemented_by`, or when the evidence does not resolve in the artifact graph (§29.13). `killed` names the
kill gate that fired and the fallback now in force. `no-claim` is a terminal status for Reality gates. The README's
honest-status table, the release rubric of §47 and the dashboard's view of gates (§32.14) are generated from this
file and from nothing else (INV-GAUNT-06; `check-generated`, §34.6). A milestone exit is a query over it.

### 38.14 Invariants and proof obligations

| Identifier | Statement | How it is checked |
|---|---|---|
| INV-GAUNT-01 | No fixture is regenerated from the candidate. | Fixtures are written only by `tools/oracle/` scripts whose inputs exclude the workspace's build products; CI fails on a fixture changed without a pin record |
| INV-GAUNT-02 | Every gate that records PASS has at least one registered control that was caught in the same run. | Schema check on `gates.toml`; the runner refuses to write `pass` otherwise |
| INV-GAUNT-03 | Agreement with an oracle is recorded with claim kind `parity`. | The record type of a G2 result has no constructor for another kind |
| INV-GAUNT-04 | Evidence computed from a fixture, a stand-in or the development oracle is labeled `synthetic` or `development-oracle`. | Label propagated through the claim product; a planted unlabeled record is refused |
| INV-GAUNT-05 | A PASS is recorded by a session that did not implement the capability. | Session identities in the record; a planted self-certification is refused |
| INV-GAUNT-06 | Status tables are generated from `registries/gates.toml` and never edited. | `check-generated` of §34.6 |
| INV-GAUNT-07 | KA, CX and control numbers are append-only. | A registry diff that reuses or renumbers an identifier fails CI |
| PO-GAUNT-01 | The paired lower bound of the harness is exact on constructed inputs. | Ten pairs of constant ratio r return r; an A/A campaign does not clear 1.02 |
| PO-GAUNT-02 | Every relation of §38.7 is broken by its registered mutation. | The mutation is applied in a test build and the relation's test must fail |
| PO-GAUNT-03 | The registry seed is complete: every CX entry and every stated negative control of Appendix M maps to a control with at least one gate. | A join over the three registries with no unmatched row |

| Gate | Claim | Threshold (TARGET) | Earliest |
|---|---|---|---|
| GATE-GAUNT-01 | Known answer | Every gate recorded as PASS has a registered negative control that it catches; a run over all controls shows each caught by every gate it names | Every milestone exit |
| GATE-GAUNT-02 | Known answer | Every row of Appendix M that is due at the milestone passes on its tier inside its tolerance, with a derivation file; no row with tolerance `to set` or control `none stated` is counted; the count of such rows is printed and is zero by M3 for rows due by M3 | Every milestone exit |
| GATE-GAUNT-03 | Known answer | Each relation of §38.7 holds on 10⁴ random scenarios per tier it names per release, and each registered mutation breaks its relation (PO-GAUNT-02) | M2, then every exit |

| GATE-GAUNT-05 | Known answer | Oracle hygiene: every fixture has a pin, a date and a capture script; a planted fixture regenerated from the candidate fails CI; no foreign tool appears in any production dependency graph; a planted G2 result offered as Reality is refused | M0, then every exit |
| GATE-GAUNT-06 | Known answer | Chaos: every row of §38.9 ends in its typed refusal or an accepted state with no leaked resource; 10⁵ seeded schedules per release; every await point on the publish and submit paths hit | M2 for kernels; M3 for the daemon |
| GATE-GAUNT-07 | Known answer | Determinism: every kernel and every verb meets its declared class twice on one machine, across worker counts, in debug and release, across platforms and across backends; MR-01 holds on 10⁴ worlds per device family | M2 |

| GATE-GAUNT-09 | Known answer | Experiment form: every registered method experiment has the six parts of §38.4; the harness passes PO-GAUNT-01; a planted experiment with an unreachable verdict is refused | M1 |

Fallback: development-oracle results lose standing as funding evidence; pilots for work packages not yet funded wait for the CPU referee, and packages funded on a reversed pilot are re-decided | M1 |

## Appendix M. Known-answer suite

This appendix is the printed form of the known-answer registry of `frob-conformance` (§38.6). It compiles every
known answer that a chapter states: 835 rows, numbered KA-001 to KA-835, grouped by chapter in the order of the
outline. A row is a case with a truth that a test can compare against, the tolerance of the comparison, the tier
and site on which it runs, and the negative control that must make it fail. The registry is the authority; this
table is generated from it and is never edited by hand (ADR-GAUNT-02).

### M.1 How to read the suite

**Columns.**

| Column | Content |
|---|---|
| KA | The row's number. Numbers are append-only: a row is never renumbered and a number is never reused (INV-GAUNT-07) |
| Where stated | The subsection or construction that states the case, with the invariant, proof obligation or gate that owns it where the chapter names one. The mark "≡ KA-nnn" means the row uses the same fixture as an earlier row in another chapter (ADR-GAUNT-06): one tests the mathematics, the other the subsystem. Marks are printed where two rows share printed constants; the registry holds the complete map |
| The case | The fixture: bodies, parameters, inputs |
| The truth | The formula or value, with the derivation steps the chapter gives. Values are carried as the chapter prints them and are not improved in transit (§38.6, rule 5) |
| Tolerance | `exact` for integers, rationals, Booleans, verdicts, labels and digests; a stated value, with the gate that states it; "to the printed digits" for half a unit in the last digit printed; `to set` where the chapter states none (OPEN-GAUNT-2) |
| Tier | The Gauntlet tier and the execution site, as `G<k> · <site>`. Tiers are those of §38.2. Sites: `host` (exact or double-precision arithmetic in a test), `auditor` (`frob-verify`), `referee`, `strict` (`batch-cpu` under the `strict` profile), `lockstep` (the throughput tiers under the `lockstep` profile), `cuda`, `metal`, `daemon`, `all tiers`. "Synthetic" and "simulated" qualify a site that runs on planted data or under the simulation runtime |
| Negative control | The mutation or planted failure that the row must catch, as the chapter states it; `none stated` where it states none (OPEN-GAUNT-3) |

**Labels.** Fixture constants are DESIGN choices. A truth derived from them by hand is DESIGN arithmetic with a
derivation file under `docs/derivations/`. A truth that quotes a planning pilot, a sibling's source or a publication
is FACT, to be re-run or re-read from its primary source before a gate cites it; such rows say so. A threshold
inside a row (a pass count, a percentage, a bound of a gate) is the TARGET of the gate named beside it. No row is a
measurement of this project: nothing here has been run on FrankenRobotics code.

**Rules.**

1. A row runs first on the weakest site that can decide it and then on every tier its gate names.
2. A row whose tier is G3 is a relation with an exact expected equality; it is also registered in §38.7. A row
   whose tier is G4 or G5 has a truth of the form "zero violations" or "equal digests".
3. A fixture stated by a construction of §10 and again by its owning chapter has a row in each place. The
   derivation file is shared.
4. A row printed with `to set` or `none stated` is an obligation, not a test: it cannot support a gate until the
   missing part is registered (GATE-GAUNT-02).
5. A row whose printed value disagrees with its own formula keeps the printed value and is listed in M.38.

By tier: G0 135 rows, G1 591, G2 16, G3 36, G4 19, G5 38. Rows that share a fixture with an earlier row in
another chapter and are marked "≡": 14.

Gaps, each an obligation on the owning chapter and none a defect of the suite's arithmetic:

- **Controls.** 325 rows state no negative control. A further handful state a boundary behaviour where a mutation is
  needed: "a 4 mm film is refused" (§19.18), "with a 29 m budget the witness is rejected" and "a tree refuses
  localization" (§20.20). OPEN-GAUNT-3.
- **Tolerances.** 133 rows state no tolerance, or only part of one, for a real-valued truth. OPEN-GAUNT-2.
- **Truths named and not written.** §14 to §16 name closed forms for the pendulum period, the precession of a top,
  the pole of the two-wheeled balancer and the hydraulic cylinder, and do not write them; the rows that cite them
  carry no value until the derivation files exist. §14.13 sends the track rejection fixture to a belt-law known
  answer of §18 that §18.16 does not list under that name; the nearest row is the skid-steer base under exact
  no-slip, with yaw rate zero.
- **Chapters without rows.** §3, §5 and §6 state no known answers.
- **Illustrative cards.** Two rows of §15 and §18 serve one formula with different illustrative cards (a backlash
  gap closing at 0.577 rad/s with 0.0115 J against 17.5 ms and 0.083 rad/s; a critical current of 31.9 A against
  35.8 A in C-38, where G = 0.5 W/K). Both are internally consistent; the registry keeps both fixtures under
  separate identities.

The conclusion (the test had no power) is unchanged |
| 2 | §2.3, §36.4 | "About 7.8×" at a step share of 87% | 1/(1 − 0.87) = 7.69 | 7.8 follows from the unrounded share 82/94 (94/12 = 7.83) |
| 3 | §36.3, row 4 | 515 s as 1.25 times the published 410 s | 1.25 × 410 = 512.5 s | Rounding of a planning value; the binding gate is the ratio 1.25 against a re-measured incumbent (GATE-CORTEX-05) |
| 4 | §19.10.3 | 352 film tiles for a 6 m × 6 m floor, and 16 tiles (about 16 kB) for 1 m² | Area over tile area gives 351.6 and 9.8 tiles; tile-aligned counts give 361 and 16 | Two rounding rules in one paragraph |
| 5 | §19.15 | "About 190 GB … a little under 85%" of about 222 GiB | 85% of 222 GiB is 188.7 GiB, or 202.6 GB; 190 GB is 79.7% of 222 GiB | Gigabytes and gibibytes mixed; §36.10 sets the device budget at 80% |

## Appendix N. Counterexample catalogue

The plan's rule is that a plausible shortcut ships with the smallest hand-checkable case that breaks it (§3, §9).
This appendix compiles every such case that a chapter states: 384 entries, numbered CX-001 to CX-384, grouped by
chapter in the order of the outline. Each entry is the seed of at least one record in the negative-control registry
(§38.11): the shortcut is planted as a mutation or as an input, and the gates of the rule it protects must catch
it.

### N.1 How to read the catalogue

| Column | Content |
|---|---|
| CX | The entry's number, append-only (INV-GAUNT-07) |
| The tempting shortcut | The argument or implementation choice that looks right |
| The smallest case that breaks it | The case, with its numbers, as the chapter states it. "No numeric case is given in the chapter" marks an entry whose refutation is stated in words only |
| The rule it protects | The invariant, decision, proof obligation, refusal code, gate or construction rule that exists because of the case |
| Defined in | The subsection or construction that states it. The mark "≡ CX-nnn" means the entry uses the same fixture as an earlier entry in another chapter |

**Rules.**

1. The same case may appear in more than one chapter, each time protecting a different rule: the two-latch task
   protects a construction in §10, an analysis in the body chapters and the controller in §22. Each statement keeps its
   entry, because the registry holds one control per protected rule and each must be caught by that rule's gates.
2. A case is carried with the chapter's numbers. Where a number is FACT from a planning pilot or from a sibling's
   source, the entry says so in its text or its chapter does; such a case is re-run in this repository before it is
   registered as a control.
3. An entry with no numeric case is an obligation on its chapter: a control needs a concrete planted defect or
   input (N.35).
4. A counterexample found in use (a false certificate, a false promotion, a withdrawn result) is appended here and
   becomes a permanent control (§25.5.5, §38.11).

### N.2 Part 0. Document control

| CX | The tempting shortcut | The smallest case that breaks it | The rule it protects | Defined in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| CX-001 | Run a speed comparison first and account for the shared work afterwards | A specialized solver measured 1.345× a composed one (1.088× cold). Shared authoring, checking and serialization were 94% of the batch, so no solver could have shown more than 1.46×: the test could not have confirmed the claim it was run to test | The ceiling implied by the work both arms share is computed before a speed comparison is locked, and the test is refused if the ceiling is below the claimed ratio (D-10) | §0.7; §2.3 item 6; §2.5 |
| CX-002 | Treat a closed bead as an accepted capability | A sibling project (FrankenMining) recorded 87 beads closed within 12 seconds with one shared close reason, serving figures from a stand-in | A bead closes with a typed reason that cites its own test, and gates require a live-dependency proof (§38) | §0.7 |

### N.3 §2 The ten-fold thesis

| CX | The tempting shortcut | The smallest case that breaks it | The rule it protects | Defined in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| CX-003 | Declare ten-fold when the point estimate is at least 10 on every brief | A draft rule of that form confirmed a true ratio of 10 in at most 18% of simulated campaigns | The decision rule is specified on a pooled ratio with at least ten paired campaigns per arm, and its power against a true ratio of 10 is computed before the sealed suite is opened (§39) | §2.1 |
| CX-004 | Seek the ten-fold in a faster simulator step | At a fixed sample count an infinitely fast environment step speeds training by at most 1/(1 − s): about 7.8× for a rough-terrain humanoid (s = 87%), 6.7× for a hand, 1.9× for a cart-pole, and about 1.4× from physics alone on another stack | Engine throughput is a parity requirement and not the source of the ten-fold (§36) | §2.3 item 1 |
| CX-005 | Decide one design per agent turn and rely on a fast cycle | With the same agent latency t in both arms and about 300 s of incumbent training, one design per turn gives at most (300 + t)/t: 3.5–6× for t between 60 and 120 s, and 4.4–7.8× against the 410 s one-card figure, even if the candidate spent no compute at all. A 120 s cycle with no training is 3.0–3.5× an incumbent quadruped cycle of 360–420 s | The unit of dispatch is a family: many designs are decided per turn (§17, §28) | §2.3 items 2 and 3 |
| CX-006 | Rely on pruning bad designs to cut compute per decision | Compute ratio C ÷ (c + c_T·f) with C = 410 GPU-s, c = 38 GPU-s, c_T = 410 GPU-s: about 10.8× at f = 0; ten needs f ≤ 0.7%; no f reaches ten if the cheap rung costs more than 41 GPU-s. If 16% of candidates fail trivially, pruning them cuts trainings by at most 1.19× | A cheaper cheap rung, many designs per rung, or a cheaper training rung is required; static checks are valued for feedback in seconds, not for pruning | §2.2; §2.3 item 4; §2.5 |
| CX-007 | Multiply a pruning ratio by a GPU speedup | If a fraction f of the strong baseline's matched wall time is improved by a factor r with overhead h, the speedup is at most 1/(1 − f + f/r + h): about 13.5× at f = 0.97, r = 40, h = 0.02, and infinite acceleration of 85% of the time cannot reach ten | The three terms are measured on the rival's critical path, and a pruning ratio is never multiplied by a speedup measured on work the pruning already removed (D-10) | §2.3 item 5 |
| CX-008 | Report speed on the missions that finished | If the candidate misses a fraction q of deadlines then R ≤ 1/q: with 12% unfinished the ratio cannot exceed 25/3 ≈ 8.33, and an arm that leaves one mission in eight unresolved cannot show ten-fold | Restricted mean time charges the deadline H for an unresolved mission; a failure is a failed cell and never an excluded cell (§8.3 rule 2) | §2.1; §2.3 item 7 |
| CX-009 | Use a sampling controller as the evaluator because it needs no training | The most sample-efficient published controller costs about 4 × 10³ times a policy rollout per simulated second: a battery of 256 twenty-second episodes takes about 86 minutes of a device against about 1.3 s for a policy | The sampling controller is a fallback and a source of demonstrations and is never the evaluator of record (BET-27) | §2.5 |

| CX | The tempting shortcut | The smallest case that breaks it | The rule it protects | Defined in |
|---|---|---|---|---|

Numbers (same case as the C-17 row): (e, W) = (0, 1.9) and (0.9, 1) each need 0.95 N; the midpoint needs 841/800 N from a pair capped at 1 N | Region certificates need a witness for the whole cell (BET-20) | §4 BET-20; numbers in C-17 |
| CX-011 | Coupled-bus fixture: merge two modules because their local summaries are identical | §4 states the control: two modules with identical local summaries share one current-limited bus and must not be merged. Numbers (same case as the C-12 row): on a unit-current bus no one-tick schedule is feasible and two two-tick schedules are | Campaign quotienting makes no false equivalence; one false equivalence falsifies it (BET-31) | §4 BET-31; numbers in C-12 |
| CX-012 | Pre-slip aliasing fixture: enforce a safety budget with a monitor alone | §4 states the control: two friction regimes with identical histories before first slip, where a monitor alone cannot see the breach in time. §4 gives no numbers for it; the numeric instance is with the energy-budget tier (C-31, §22) | The energy-budget claim is enforced by a shield, not only observed by a monitor (BET-25) | §4 BET-25 |
| CX-013 | Omitted-law fixture: issue a calibration band because every in-family residual check passes | A fixture with an omitted law passes every in-family residual check and reverses the decision; in the recorded pilot the probe was correct in 675 of 675 in-class cases and in 0 of 225 on the omitted law | The exchangeability alarm or a refusal must fire; a band is issued only within a calibrated family (BET-28) | §4 BET-28 |

| CX | The tempting shortcut | The smallest case that breaks it | The rule it protects | Defined in |
|---|---|---|---|---|

The product is a Design comparison of class Estimated, its weakest input is the friction field, and its reality scope is `no-claim` | The weakest input wins by construction of the claim-product type; no conversion into Proved, no setter for the class, no constructor that takes a class (§8.3 rule 5) | §8.2 |

Numbers (same case as the C-17 row): docking with a late packet, gains 1 and 2, whose required commands differ by ½ | Quantifiers are part of the claim; evidence for the easier order is rejected, and the auditor rejects a changed quantifier even when every hash is consistent | §8.3 rule 8; §4 BET-19; numbers in C-17, C-19 |

### N.6 §9 Doctrine

| CX | The tempting shortcut | The smallest case that breaks it | The rule it protects | Defined in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| CX-016 | Assume a specialized method beats the generic one | On the static-stance family of C-32 a lifted robust linear program and the specialized projection each reached the optimum in one solve (recorded pilot, to be re-run in this repository before a gate cites it) | A specialized method names the generic method it must beat; if the generic method ties, the plan says so and ships the generic method (D-17) | §9 D-17 |
| CX-017 | Decide a strict predicate in unguarded single precision | An unguarded `f32` screen of 65,536 boundary cases produced 452 false admissions and 23,438 false exclusions; a guarded or exact-integer kernel produced none (recorded pilot, same caveat) | Strict predicates, digests and certificate arithmetic run in the exact-integer kernel class, in exact rationals, or in guarded single precision with an exact fallback (D-18; BET-08; §35) | §9 D-18; §4 BET-08 |
| CX-018 | Leave a model assumption in the prose around a claim | Changing one winding feed-forward term in a sampled wheel controller moved a one-tick spectral radius from 1.48 to 0.92 and changed 24 of 156 task labels; admitting one extra hidden gain reversed every feasible label on the pilot's grid of the docking fixture (recorded pilots, same caveat) | A model assumption is an input with a name: actuator authority, load-sharing law, hidden-parameter support, delivery deadline and controller information are fields of the claim (D-19) | §9 D-19 |

### N.7 §10 The constructions

| CX | The tempting shortcut | The smallest case that breaks it | The rule it protects | Defined in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| CX-019 | Compute mobility from the Grübler–Kutzbach count | Sarrus linkage: the count gives 0 and the mobility is 1. Bennett linkage: the count gives −2 and the mobility is 1 | Mobility comes from intersections and products in the subgroup lattice, cross-checked by the rank of the constraint Jacobian at exact rational sample points (A-01) | C-01 |
| CX-020 | Move every spatial quantity with one adjoint, so that a wrench is moved by the twist map | A twist moves by Ad_g; a wrench is a covector and moves by Ad_g⁻ᵀ (pushforward) or Ad_gᵀ (pullback). A single reversed adjoint must fail three independent fixtures: Ad(g·h) = Ad(g)·Ad(h), frame invariance of the power pairing, and the planar 2R fixture of C-04 (torques −3 N·m, power −0.3 W) | Three separately typed maps; a wrench cannot be moved by the twist map (T-04, T-05; §11.3) | C-02 (numeric statement of the maps in §11.3) |
| CX-021 | Accept an inertia because it passes the positivity and triangle tests | A centred 1 kg part with three principal moments of 0.008 kg·m² passes both, yet tr(I_C)/(2m) = 0.012 m² where a ball of radius 0.10 m allows 0.010 m². An isotropic thin shell of that radius, with moments 1/150 kg·m², is the equality case | The mass-support bound ‖c − o‖² + tr(I_C)/(2m) ≤ ρ² for a declared envelope | C-03 |
| CX-022 | Derive inertia from geometry by filling every enclosure solid | An aluminium box of 0.4 × 0.3 × 0.2 m at 2,700 kg/m³ is 64.8 kg as a solid and 3.45 kg with a 2.5 mm wall, a ratio of 18.8 | A declared wall thickness makes a body a shell by the difference rule; a solid fill of a large enclosure raises a density warning (§11.5, FROB-REF-168) | C-03 |
| CX-023 | Take the lower three rows of the space Jacobian as the point Jacobian | Planar 2R chain, links 0.4 m and 0.3 m, angles 0 and π/2, tool point (0.4, 0.3) m: the point Jacobian is [[−0.3, −0.3], [0.4, 0.0]] m/rad, a tool force (10, 0) N gives torques (−3, −3) N·m, and both power computations give −0.3 W. For a space column (ωᵢ, vᵢ) the point column is vᵢ + ωᵢ × p; the lower rows alone drop ωᵢ × p and fail the fixture | The space Jacobian pairs with the wrench (p × F, F) about the origin and the point Jacobian pairs with F (D-9) | C-04 |
| CX-024 | Treat a limb's articulated inertia at its attachment port as positive definite and solve the limbs independently | Eliminating a joint axis s gives I − I·s·(sᵀ·I·s)⁻¹·sᵀ·I; for I the identity and s the first axis the result is diag(0, 1, 1, 1, 1, 1): one direction is removed, so the packet is semidefinite | Multi-limb ground contact goes through the coupled base solve | C-04 |
| CX-025 | Use the Cayley update as if it were the exponential at any step size | The Cayley step rotates by 2·atan(‖ω‖·h/2) instead of ‖ω‖·h; it is second-order | Use is bounded by ‖ω‖·h ≤ 0.2 per substep and refused beyond (FROB-REF-212; GB-01) | C-05 |
| CX-026 | Approximate the friction cone by a pyramid | The box abs(γₜ₁) ≤ μ·γₙ, abs(γₜ₂) ≤ μ·γₙ admits (μ·γₙ, μ·γₙ), of norm √2·μ·γₙ: 41% of traction that does not exist along the diagonals | The second-order cone is used; the planted pyramid exploit must be caught by the model-form check | C-06 |
| CX-027 | Read full bracket rank as reachability | ẋ = u with u ∈ [0, 1]: the rank is one, and negative x is still unreachable from zero | A bracket direction is infinitesimal and assumes reversible inputs; a finite mission needs an integrated witness | C-07 |
| CX-028 | Apply ideal rolling to a four-wheel fixed-axis (skid-steer) base | With axles at ±a, lateral no-slip requires v_y + a·ω = 0 and v_y − a·ω = 0, which forces ω = 0: under the shortcut the base cannot yaw at all | Ideal rolling is a regime; yaw of such a base exists only under a separately qualified slip model | C-07 |
| CX-029 | Use a sibling routine as an oracle because it exists | `fs-mbd` `planar_drive` (reported, to recheck): from heading 32 rad with wheel speeds 1 and 1 + 6·10⁻¹³ m/s on a 0.5 m track, 400 steps of 1/400 s return zero translation where the limit is about (0.8342, 0.5514) m | A foundation is qualified against an independent formula at admission before it serves as an oracle; a reported defect becomes an admission test and an upstream ask (§33; BET-04) | C-07; §1.7 |
| CX-030 | Write the curvature of the mechanical connection with +[𝔸ᵢ, 𝔸ⱼ] | On the planar three-link floating body, the component formula with the bracket term's sign flipped fails the loop test against direct integration by an error of order one | The component formula and its sign are fixed once (§11.6) and carry a sign control | C-08 (formula in §11.6) |
| CX-031 | Reduce by the mechanical symmetry group Γ_mech where the observation-preserving group Γ_obs is required | A quadruped with a front-only lidar has Γ_mech = C₂ × C₂ and Γ_obs = C₂. Adding a one-sided payload removes the reflection from Γ_obs. A quadruped crossing a one-sided slippery patch must be allowed asymmetric actions | Exact reduction uses only Γ_obs intersected with the stabilizers of payload, boundary conditions and contact mode (D-14) | C-09 |
| CX-032 | Treat angular velocity and torque as polar vectors under a mirror | Under the sagittal mirror P = diag(1, −1, 1) the axial image of a joint axis a is −P·a: a pitch axis along y keeps its sign, and roll and yaw axes change theirs. A table that flips all three, or none, fails the step-equivariance test | Polar vectors map by P and axial vectors by det(P)·P (§11.4; T-10) | C-09 |
| CX-033 | Conclude that a task is infeasible because the search over symmetry-tied deterministic policies failed | Two identical latches, exactly one must close. At a reflection-fixed observation a tied deterministic policy issues equal commands and both fail; either asymmetric choice succeeds, and a policy that draws one of the two uniformly is equivariant in law | A failure of tied-policy search excludes only the tied policy class, and the report says so and nothing more | C-09 |
| CX-034 | Find a gait orbit inside Fix(K) and judge its stability there | An orbit computed inside Fix(K) can hide unstable multipliers transverse to it (C-10 states the rule; the smallest numeric case is given with the gait analysis of §22) | The monodromy is always computed on the full space | C-10 |
| CX-035 | Refuse simultaneous impacts, or impose an order on them | Trot, bound and pronk have simultaneous touchdowns by construction; forcing an order can change the outgoing velocity and a gait's basin | Simultaneous events are resolved by one coupled solve and are never a reason to refuse a gait | C-10; C-16 |
| CX-036 | Summarize a port by its peak demand and add the peaks | Two fingers on one unit-current bus have identical local summaries.
