A rule that adds their peak demands rejects a task that a two-tick schedule completes: no one-tick schedule is feasible and two two-tick schedules are | A port contract carries time correlation; the two-finger bus fixture must not be pruned, and two modules with identical local summaries on one current-limited bus are not merged (BET-31) | C-12 |
| CX-037 | Reuse a result after an edit because the sub-term's hash still matches | Swapping a leaf keeps its siblings' local results, but an edit that touches a shared battery bus, a shared thermal sink, a cross-branch kinematic loop or base-coupled contact invalidates every global result, because a shared moving base couples all limbs through the contact operator. Controls: a mirror swap of a leaf reuses its siblings' results; an edit to the shared bus must invalidate | A hash identifies; only a recorded implication licenses skipping a recheck (D-11; FROB-REF-835) | C-12; D-11 |
| CX-038 | Score an estimator after fitting a scale | A 2 m estimate of a 1 m motion has zero residual after a factor of ½ | A metric gravity-referenced estimate is aligned with one translation and one yaw per trajectory and never with a fitted scale | C-13 |
| CX-039 | File a reassociation as a bit-exact rewrite | In `f32` with a = 10⁸, b = −10⁸, c = 1: (a + b) + c is 1 and a + (b + c) is 0 | Rewrite classes: class E changes no bit and ships a replayable trace; class R (reassociation, distribution, fused multiply-add) makes a different kernel with a new identity; the trace checker rejects the misfiled rewrite (T-27) | C-14 |
| CX-040 | Freeze a fixed-n decision rule without listing the verdicts it can return | Two-sided exact Clopper–Pearson rule at n = 64 with a ±5 point indifference band: perfect concordance gives [−0.0662, +0.0662], so no outcome can return a tie. The recorded rule with 64 pairs could return three of its four verdicts; the fourth was first reachable at 86 pairs | A fixed-n study ships a pre-analysis of reachable verdicts (T-26); a study that needs an unreachable verdict is refused at admission | C-15; §0.7 |
| CX-041 | Use the reset Jacobian as the derivative across an event | Bouncing ball, state (y, v), reset (y, −r·v), impact speed v⁻ < 0: the saltation matrix is [[−r, 0], [−(1 + r)·g/v⁻, −r]] with determinant r². The reset Jacobian diag(1, −r) has the wrong sign in the first entry, omits the coupling in the second row, and for r = 1 has determinant −1 where the elastic bounce preserves area with determinant +1 | Event derivatives use the saltation matrix and carry the label `saltation` (D-15) | C-16 |
| CX-042 | Transport a derivative across an event by a symmetry without checking that the relabeling commutes with the event | Two bouncing balls and the swap of their labels satisfy D h(x⁺)·Ξ_e(x⁻) = Ξ_{h·e}(h·x⁻)·D h(x⁻) when the restitutions are equal and violate it by terms proportional to r_L − r_R when they differ | A symmetry is used across an event only where the commuting identity holds | C-16 |
| CX-043 | Return a derivative at a grazing event | A ball whose apex touches a ceiling: the guard is not crossed transversally, and the query returns `refused`, not a matrix | Refusal near grazing, at an ambiguous event order, across an active-set change or at frictional degeneracy (FROB-REF-263) | C-16 |
| CX-044 | Certify the convex hull of two feasible designs | Feet at (±1 − e, ±1) m, each capped at 0.5 N, carry a load W at the origin; the positive-x pair must supply W·(1 + e)/2. The designs (e, W) = (0, 1.9) and (0.9, 1) each need 0.95 N and are feasible; their midpoint (0.45, 1.45) needs 841/800 N from a pair capped at 1 N | An inner cell needs a witness for the whole cell; only a union of proved inner cells is inner; the midpoint must be excluded from every inner set (BET-20) | C-17 ≡ CX-010 |
| CX-045 | Accept one policy per world as evidence that one policy works for all worlds (∀w ∃π offered for ∃π ∀w) | Docking fixture with the packet arriving late, gains g ∈ {1, 2}: with first command p the remaining commands must be (1/g − 2p, p − 1/g), which differ by ½ between the gains. Each world alone has a successful open-loop command and no single command succeeds in both | Quantifiers are part of the claim; evidence for an easier quantifier order is rejected by type and by the auditor (D-12; §8.3 rule 8; FROB-REF-826) | C-17; C-19 |
| CX-046 | Evaluate a strict requirement with a non-strict comparator, or by an infimum | At U = ¾ and ε = 5/16 the docking residual is exactly zero and the strict predicate fails: with p = 5/8 both worlds can report 15/16 while needing −¼ and −¾. On 0 < w ≤ 1 every realization has w > 0 although the infimum is 0 | Strict, non-strict and equality atoms are evaluated with their declared comparators; a uniform positive margin is a separate, stronger request; strict decisions are computed exactly (D-18) | C-17; C-19 |
| CX-047 | Multiply stage pass rates to get the mission pass rate | Among 20 equally likely worlds two stages each pass in 19. The mission passes in 18 worlds if the failing worlds differ and in 19 if they coincide | A chance constraint preserves the joint law across stages or allocates failure budgets that sum to the allowance; a product bound needs conditional success at every reached history | C-17 |
| CX-048 | Intersect per-world answers to obtain the joint answer | F(V) ⊆ ⋂ over w ∈ V of F({w}), and the gap is what the controller cannot observe in time: in the docking fixture with a late packet each of the worlds g = 1 and g = 2 is solvable alone and the joint query has no solution | The joint query is answered as stated; per-world evidence is not combined by intersection (D-9, D-12) | C-18 |
| CX-049 | Keep an inclusion when the world set grows | Docking family: enlarging W by one hidden gain 33/32 keeps every exclusion and forces a recheck of every inclusion. The inclusion at (U, ε) = (¾, 1/64) reverses, because the reading (65/64)·p is possible under gains 1 and 33/32 for every first command p; the least worst-case terminal error becomes 1/65 m | Verdicts have polarity: an inner certificate survives when W shrinks, Π grows or A weakens, and must be rechecked on the opposite moves; an exclusion is the reverse | C-18 |
| CX-050 | Trust a cache key or a dependency record to mean the verdict still holds | A dependency graph with one edge omitted returns a promotion that fresh replay contradicts | Reuse needs a recorded implication; the stale-verdict control must be caught by the shadow recompute (D-11; BET-33) | C-18 |
| CX-051 | Take pairwise compatibility as joint compatibility | Commands (x, y) ∈ [0, 1]² with three variants requiring x ≥ 2/3, y ≥ 2/3 and x + y ≤ 1: every pair of sets intersects and the triple does not. Pairwise compatibility would have authorized one shared controller; two labels suffice and one is impossible | The label count is a colouring of the hypergraph whose edges are the inclusion-minimal collections with empty intersection | C-19 |
| CX-052 | Count repeated sensor reads as independent evidence | A binary probe correct with probability ¾ gives 27/32 with three independent reads, 459/512 with five and 3807/4096 with seven, and stays at ¾ after any number of reads that share one fault bit | Error dependence, not per-read accuracy, sets the value of redundancy | C-19 |
| CX-053 | Expect more training to overcome a sensing limit | Docking fixture: if the only informative packet arrives after the third command is committed, no causal policy docks in both worlds for any U and ε; the two worlds deliver identical packet histories up to the commitment deadline and need disjoint commands | An indistinguishability witness ends a policy search before it is paid for (`mission.refute`) | C-19 |
| CX-054 | Specify sensor accuracy separately from actuator authority and the probing command | Docking fixture at U = ¾: the probe p = ¼ tolerates ε < 1/8 and the probe p = 5/8 tolerates ε < 5/16 on the same actuator; in general ε < (U + ½)/4 | Sensor, actuator and delivery time appear in one design inequality; the probe and the feedback are chosen together | C-19 |
| CX-055 | Read a full-rank local observability Gramian as distinguishability | Range anchors at (−1, 0) and (1, 0) both read √2 from (0, 1) and from (0, −1). With unit variances the information matrix is the identity at both points, yet a controller that must pick the side fails. A third anchor at (0, 2) reads 1 or 3, and closed reading intervals of half-width ε separate exactly when ε < 1 | Every calibration and placement analysis also runs a global ambiguity search | C-20 |
| CX-056 | Two designs whose geometric ratios match are dynamically similar, so a verdict moves between them | Scale a body by λ at constant density: mass × λ³, static joint torque × λ⁴. With a motor whose torque scales as λ³ the group τ_max/(m₀·g·ℓ₀) falls as 1/λ, so a design that stands at one size fails at twice the size. A fixed latency has delay group ∝ λ^(−½): it doubles at one quarter of the size | Every dimensionless group must match before a verdict is transported; differing groups are reported by name (C-21, D-11) | C-21 |
| CX-057 | A physical constant may sit in a kernel as a pure number | Scale lengths × 4, times × 2, masses × 2 with gravity unchanged. A homogeneous kernel reproduces every significand bit and branch; a kernel with a hidden dimensional constant differs on its first step | Dimensional homogeneity of every kernel (T-09); the power-of-two similarity test | C-21 |
| CX-058 | Quotient a campaign by the mechanical symmetry group Γ_mech of the chassis | Ten leg types on a hexapod: dihedral symmetry of order 12 gives 86,185 classes. With a front-only lidar only the sagittal mirror survives and there are 500,500 classes; counting with Γ_mech merges designs that the closed loop distinguishes | The acting group of a campaign is Γ_obs, never Γ_mech alone (C-22, C-09) | C-22 |
| CX-059 | Designs that agree on some mission observables can be merged | Eight ballast slugs enter the dynamics through count, Σx and Σx². Slots {1, 4} and {2, 3} have equal count and equal Σx = 5 but Σx² = 17 and 13: they are different rigid bodies | A projection class requires equality over all active observables, and merged pairs must replay bit-identically (C-22) | C-22 |
| CX-060 | A design-conditioned policy may read its tokens in file order | Exchange two legs in the description of one robot: the robot is unchanged and the actions change. FACT (source register): a published morphology-conditioned policy lost most of its return when the same robots were re-described in equivalent conventions | A family policy is natural under re-description, σ(d′) = φ·σ(d); permutation-equivariant tokens (C-23) | C-23 |
| CX-061 | More equivariance is better: make the policy equivariant under all rotations | A rotation about a horizontal axis does not preserve gravity. The rigid motions that do are rotations about the vertical, translations and reflections through vertical planes, and the layers are equivariant under those only | The gravity control of C-24: a horizontal-axis rotation must not be a symmetry of the policy | C-24 |
| CX-062 | Audit a small share of worlds on the high tier and correct by inverse weights; the cheap prediction only has to be roughly right | With Y ∈ {0, 1}, a prediction wrong by a constant ½ and audit probability 0.1, the added variance is (1 − p)/p·(Y − m)² = 9 × ¼ = 2.25, worse than auditing everything. The estimate stays unbiased; the cost is variance | Tier correction is funded only where the measured ρ supports it (kill below 0.7); p_min bounds the weights (C-25) | C-25 |
| CX-063 | Calibrate the conformal margin on the designs that were promoted, since those are the ones that ran both rungs | On a fixture where the residual abs(y_hi − ĝ(y_lo)) correlates with the cheap score, q̂ computed from promoted designs only under-covers | The audit set is drawn by keyed randomness before any score exists and run at fixed n (C-26) | C-26 |
| CX-064 | A dozen audit designs are enough for a 95% conformal margin | The index is ⌈(n + 1)·(1 − α)⌉: n = 39 gives the 38th smallest residual; n = 18 gives 19, which exceeds n. Fewer than 19 audit designs cannot support α = 0.05 | Minimum audit-set size for a declared α (C-26) | C-26 |
| CX-065 | A defensive mixture proposal makes certifying the absence of failures cheaper | With q = β·p + (1 − β)·q′ the weights are at most 1/β, and zero observed failures certify only p ≤ ln(1/δ)/(β·n). At δ = 0.05 and p* = 10⁻⁴ crude sampling needs about 3·10⁴ nominal episodes, and the mixture needs that many on its nominal share | Certification and exploration are two lanes that never mix (D-16); no sampler discounts certified absence (C-27) | C-27 |
| CX-066 | Forking a world at a splitting level multiplies the evidence | A fork without fresh randomness is an identical copy: children that share the parent's streams reproduce the parent's trajectory bit for bit and add nothing | Child streams are keyed by child world addresses; duplicate children are detected (C-27, §11.1) | C-27 |
| CX-067 | The shrunk failing world is the smallest one, and its size measures distance to failure | Edit 1 (a grease patch) fails the design; edit 2 (a rug over the patch) masks it. {1} fails and {1, 2} passes, so failure is not monotone in the edit set. With eight edits and failure exactly when 3 and 6 are present the search returns {3, 6}, which is 1-minimal by construction and nothing more | A result is reported as 1-minimal under the tested edit relation; no distance to a boundary is claimed (C-28, §12.3) | C-28 |
| CX-068 | Any world in which the design fails is a counterexample | A doorway narrower than the body fails every controller: it is a useless trap and says nothing about the design | A counterexample is kept only with a solvability witness from the candidate's own body, actuators and mission constraints (C-28) | C-28 |
| CX-069 | A large Shapley value identifies a necessary cause | Two interventions A and B. Case 1: either alone prevents the failure, v({A}) = v({B}) = v({A, B}) = 1. Case 2: only both together prevent it, v({A}) = v({B}) = 0, v({A, B}) = 1. Both give φ = (½, ½); case 1 has two but-for causes with PN = 1 and case 2 has none | A cause is named by the probability of necessity against the placebo flip rate; Shapley is a budgeted summary (C-29) | C-29 |
| CX-070 | An intervention that flips the outcome is a cause | On a fixture where every last-place nudge of an unrelated state flips the outcome, any intervention appears to prevent the failure | The placebo fork: c is a cause only when PN(c) exceeds the placebo flip rate ρ₀; otherwise the report is `chaotic: unattributable` (C-29) | C-29 |
| CX-071 | A design-parameter change can be tested by forking at a snapshot just before the failure | For a gear ratio or a mass, the thermal state, the estimator state and the policy's history at the snapshot would all differ under the changed design, and an intervention at a snapshot answers a question about the continuation only | Design-parameter interventions re-run from the episode start on the same streams; a fork is refused when the controller cannot adapt without retraining (C-29) | C-29 |
| CX-072 | The robustness of a conjunction is the minimum of the raw margins | A clearance of 0.02 m and a thermal margin of 15 K: the minimum is 0.02 (clearance binds) in metres and kelvin, and 15 (the thermal margin binds) in millimetres and kelvin. With declared scales 0.10 m and 10 K the values are 0.2 and 1.5 and clearance binds in every unit system | Every atom has a declared scale of its own dimension; robustness is ρ = ψ/s (C-30) | C-30 |
| CX-073 | When an episode ends early, evaluate "always" over the samples that exist | An episode that ends at 30 s under G_[0, 45 s]: the minimum over 30 s can be positive although the requirement was never tested on the last 15 s | A window the trace does not cover yields `inconclusive`, never a truncated minimum (C-30) | C-30 |
| CX-074 | Project the action onto the barrier row, then clip to the actuator box | R = I, u_π = (1, 0), box [0, 1]², row u₁ + u₂ ≥ 1.5. Projection ignoring the box gives (1.25, 0.25), outside the box; clipping gives (1, 0.25), with u₁ + u₂ = 1.25 < 1.5. The breakpoint method gives (1, 0.5) | The one-row filter is solved exactly inside the box; multi-row solves carry a mandatory KKT check with a passive fallback (C-31) | C-31 |
| CX-075 | For every disturbance there is a safe action, so the filter is safe | A certificate proved in the order ∀w ∃u lets the action be chosen after the disturbance and the estimation error are seen, which the controller cannot do | The quantifier order is ∃u ∀(w, e); a certificate in the other order is rejected (C-31, FROB-REF-826) | C-31 |
| CX-076 | An energy budget may leave out small sources | Lifting 8 kg over a 12 mm sill needs 8 × 9.81 × 0.012 = 0.94176 J, above a 0.90 J budget, so the family is excluded; 0.05 J of approach kinetic energy (0.95 J) removes the exclusion. In the stop budget (0.64 + 0.50 + 0.25 + 0.10 = 1.49 J of 2.00 J) a missing gravity term over the stroke makes the checker reject | A budget has a term for every source on the declared load path (T-28); the omitted-term control | C-31 |
| CX-077 | The first moment of the load can be passed where the support torque is expected | With +z up the support torque is (P_y, −P_x, 0). P = (60, 30) N·m with the stance forces gives (30, −60, 0) N·m; a packet that passes (60, 30) as the torque has the wrong components and sign | Every field of a bundle is typed with frame, origin, sign and unit (§12.4, FROB-REF-829) | C-32 |
| CX-078 | An inclusion proved for independently commanded foot forces holds for the same robot on a passive suspension | Stance fixture at b = ¼ m with a 160 N cap: active allocation gives (155, 135, 105, 5) N, inside the cap; equal passive springs give (175, 115, 85, 25) N, above it | A typed result states its authority model; the assumption `authority` travels with the certificate (C-32) | C-32 |
| CX-079 | Centre of mass inside the support polygon and total foot capacity above the weight means the stance is feasible | Stance fixture at b = 1/5 m: the centre of mass (150 mm, 75 mm) lies inside the feet at (±1/3, ±1/5) m and total capacity is 4 × 160 = 640 N against 400 N, yet the moment equations give f₊₊ − f₋₋ = 165 N with f₊₊ ≤ 160 N and f₋₋ ≥ 0. FACT (planning pilot, to be re-run): the test accepted 1,596 bodies the exact model rejects | The stance family is decided by the exact model with per-foot bounds and a dual witness (C-32, T-22) | C-32 |
| CX-080 | A region certificate can be had by paving the whole design box | 10⁴ boxes are 13 bisections, less than one cut per axis in 20 dimensions: a uniform box keeps 0.64 of each side (0.41 in 10 dimensions) | Certificates are issued per constraint on sub-boxes of about six parameters or fewer and assembled by products, monotonicity and witnesses (C-32, C-17) | C-32 |
| CX-081 | Clearance can be tested with one scalar radius | A thin rod of length L turns a right-angle corner between corridors of width a exactly when L ≤ 2√2·a. The circumscribed-disc test, L ≤ a, refuses every rod between a and 2.83·a although each passes; the inscribed-disc test accepts rods longer than 2.83·a, which jam | Clearance is certified from both sides on complexes in SE(2), with a reported band (C-33) | C-33 |
| CX-082 | A linkage that closes at every target pose reaches them all | Links 4 (ground), 1 (crank), 3.5 (coupler), 3 (rocker): 1 + 4 ≤ 3.5 + 3, so the crank turns fully and the two assembly circuits are disjoint. A candidate whose target poses lie on different circuits satisfies closure at each pose and cannot move between them without disassembly | The circuit and branch certificate of lane 1 (C-34) | C-34 |
| CX-083 | Every tracked root passed the α-test, so the solution set is complete | An α-test proves a nearby nonsingular root and nothing about completeness. The Stewart–Gough forward problem has 40 solutions: a run that certifies fewer, or loses a path, has not found them all | A result is `Complete` only when the certified count equals an independent generic count with no lost path; otherwise `Partial(certified, lost)` (C-34) | C-34 |
| CX-084 | Rainflow counting can be run window by window | The history 0, 10, 0 split into the windows [0, 10] and [10, 0] holds one full cycle of range 10; a counter that drops residues at window ends counts none | The residue of open half-cycles is carried between windows (C-35) | C-35 |
| CX-085 | Rare overload cycles can be dropped from a fatigue history | With m = 3, 10⁶ cycles at a life of 2·10⁶ give D = 0.5; 10³ cycles at five times the amplitude (life 16,000) add 0.0625: 0.1% of the cycles carry 11% of the damage | Load cases are the recorded envelope over qualified missions; the damage sum keeps every cycle (C-35) | C-35 |
| CX-086 | Momentum conservation is the test of a step for any system with a symmetry | Chaplygin sleigh: v̇ = a·ω², ω̇ = −m·a·v·ω/(I + m·a²). The energy ½·m·v² + ½·(I + m·a²)·ω² is constant, since m·v·(a·ω²) − m·a·v·ω·ω = 0, while the direction of linear momentum turns | The momentum test of C-04 is not applied to nonholonomic systems; they are tested by energy (C-36) | C-36 |
| CX-087 | If the variational step conserves momentum, its implicit solve is right | Evaluate step 2 with the differential at +hξ_k instead of −hξ_k (the other trivialization). Momentum stays exact, because the transport of step 3 conserves it for any ξ; the energy bound and reversibility are lost | The variational step is tested by bounded energy error (÷4 when h is halved) and by reversal, never by momentum alone (C-36) | C-36 |
| CX-088 | Model every wheel as an ideal rolling constraint | Four fixed wheels as ideal rows return ω = 0 under any command: a skid-steer base cannot turn. With cone-bounded rows it turns with its lateral multipliers on their cones | Rolling rows carry traction limits; ideal rolling is a limit of the same solve (C-36, C-07) | C-36 |
| CX-089 | Sliding friction and torsional friction are independent rows with their own coefficients | At v_t = r·ω_n the coupled law gives a tangential friction magnitude of μ·λ_n/√2; the uncoupled model gives μ·λ_n. A spinning contact resists sliding less | One four-dimensional cone for sliding and spin (C-37) | C-37 |
| CX-090 | Heat entering the winding is a loss, and the node's temperature rise is stored energy | A ledger that books the same heat as both stored and lost shows a deficit equal to that heat | H + U changes only by port power and by the heat that leaves to ambient; ledger closure in all three modes (C-38) | C-38 |
| CX-091 | A passing second-law check shows the thermal model is right | The check has content only as a sign check on each assigned loss: it cannot fail for a correct model. Its one catch: a regenerative mode with a negated copper loss gives σ < 0 | The entropy balance is a derived report, used as a sign control and not as validation (C-38) | C-38 |
| CX-092 | Any measurement of the mechanism will identify it; fit a richer curve | Laws μ₁(v) = 0.5 and μ₂(v) = 0.3 + 0.4·v agree at 0.5 m/s and imply opposite designs at the mission speed of 1.5 m/s (0.5 against 0.9). A probe at 0.5 m/s separates nothing at any precision; a probe at 1.0 m/s reads 0.5 or 0.7 and separates them exactly when the reading error is below 0.1 | A probe is chosen so that the reading sets of the decision blocks are disjoint over the nuisance intervals (C-39) | C-39 |
| CX-093 | A probe that separates the admitted mechanisms settles the design decision | A third law that follows μ₁ up to 1.0 m/s and departs later defeats the 1.0 m/s probe. FACT (planning pilot, to be re-run): a probe synthesized for three admitted laws chose correctly in 675 of 675 in-class cases; a fourth law reversed all 225 held-out decisions; direct measurement at mission dwell was right in 900 of 900 | A probe certificate is conditional on the admitted set ℳ and says so; direct measurement at mission conditions is always an admitted probe (C-39) | C-39 |
| CX-094 | Dominance can be checked by enclosing each design's outcomes separately and comparing | z ∈ [0, 1]; A succeeds when z ≥ 3/5 and B when z ≥ 2/5. Separate enclosures give {0, 1} for each and admit all four pairs, including (success, failure), which no shared z produces | Dominance is unreachability of (success, failure) in the product system on shared inputs (C-40) | C-40 |
| CX-095 | A product enclosure that covers each design's outcome set covers the joint outcomes | A product whose internal branches are wrongly tied reaches only (0, 0) and (1, 1). Its projections cover both marginal outcome sets, and it hides a reachable (1, 0) | The checker demands joint coverage; one mislabeled branch invalidates the certificate (C-40) | C-40 |
| CX-096 | Pair two designs by seed and tick, and the worlds match | Two tyres on different paths over a patterned floor meet different friction at the same tick. A product coupled by tick and not by position reports a false regression or hides a true one | Shared inputs are compiled at physical coordinates: one space-indexed field read at each robot's contact point (C-40) | C-40 |
| CX-097 | "B never loses to A" means B meets the mission | In the same fixture neither design is guaranteed: A fails for z < 3/5 and B for z < 2/5, while the paired verdict of no loss holds on the whole interval | The answer carries the paired verdict and each design's absolute verdict separately (C-40) | C-40 |

### N.8 §11 Conventions

| CX | The tempting shortcut | The smallest case that breaks it | The rule it protects | Defined in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| CX-098 | The coadjoint in the Euler–Poincaré equation carries a minus sign: μ̇ = −ad_ξᵀ μ | Force-free body with the origin at the centre of mass. With ad*_ξ = ad_ξᵀ: v̇ = v × ω, so d(R·v)/dt = R·(ω × v + v̇) = 0 and the world-frame velocity is constant. With the opposite sign d(R·v)/dt = 2R·(ω × v): a free body changes its velocity with no force | ad*_ξ = ad_ξᵀ with μ̇ = ad*_ξ μ + w; the map −ad_ξᵀ is never written ad* (§11.2, T-16, PO-MARROW-01) | §11.2 |
| CX-099 | A wrench changes frame by the same matrix as a twist | R = I, p = (1, 0, 0) m, wrench (0, (0, 0, 1) N) in b. The pushforward Ad_g⁻ᵀ gives torque p × f = (0, −1, 0) N·m and the same force. The twist map Ad_g gives zero torque: the moment arm is lost, and the planar fixture of C-04 fails | Three typed frame maps, `TwistMap`, `WrenchPushforward`, `WrenchPullback`; no function accepts one where another is expected (§11.3) | §11.3 |
| CX-100 | Angular velocity mirrors like any other vector | Sagittal mirror P = diag(1, −1, 1). Axial vectors map by det(P)·P = −P: the yaw axis e_z goes to −e_z, so a robot turning left becomes a robot turning right, and the pitch axis e_y maps to itself. Treated as polar, the yaw rate is left unchanged | Polar vectors map by P and axial vectors by det(P)·P; the step-equivariance test of T-10 (§11.4) | §11.4 |
| CX-101 | A body with no declared wall thickness can default to a solid of its outer shape | A thin spherical shell of radius r has I = (2/3)·m·r², a solid ball (2/5)·m·r²; for an enclosure the solid default is wrong in mass by an order of magnitude (C-03 gives a case with a factor of 18.8) | The shell rule; a solid body above the plausibility bound of its part class is refused unless the fill is declared (FROB-REF-168, §11.5) | §11.5 |
| CX-102 | The curvature of the connection is ∂ᵢ𝔸ⱼ − ∂ⱼ𝔸ᵢ + [𝔸ᵢ, 𝔸ⱼ] | Constant connection 𝔸₁ = e_x, 𝔸₂ = e_y on SO(3): the curl vanishes, (D𝔸)₁₂ = −[e_x, e_y] = −e_z, and a small loop of area da rotates the body by +da about z, as exp(−a·e_x)·exp(−b·e_y)·exp(a·e_x)·exp(b·e_y) ≈ exp(a·b·e_z) confirms. The + formula predicts −da, wrong by twice the answer | (D𝔸)ᵢⱼ = ∂ᵢ𝔸ⱼ − ∂ⱼ𝔸ᵢ − [𝔸ᵢ, 𝔸ⱼ] with ξ = −𝔸(s)·ṡ (§11.6, T-20; the wrong-sign control of C-08) | §11.6 |
| CX-103 | A 30 Hz policy on 400 Hz physics can run every thirteenth physics step | L = 1,200 base units per second; physics every 3 units, policy every 40. The policy tick at unit 40 falls one unit after the physics boundary at 39 and two before the one at 42; thirteen steps are 39 units, a period of 32.5 ms instead of 33⅓ ms | Rational periods with integer phase accumulators; a process need not be a multiple of the physics step, and no period is rounded (§11.7) | §11.7 |
| CX-104 | A force sensor reading is the force at the sample instant, and an impact on a window boundary can go to either window | An impact of 0.2 N·s inside a 10 ms exposure contributes 20 N to that window's average and nothing to its neighbour; an impact at exactly instant b belongs to the window ending at b | A reading is a functional of the history over the half-open window (a, b]; each impulse is added once and divided by the exposure (§11.8) | §11.8 |
| CX-105 | A decision may use any packet whose timestamp is earlier than the decision | The timestamp is a label written on the sensor's own clock and is unconstrained by causality. In the docking fixture of C-19 the same packet is on time when its arrival precedes the second decision and worthless when it follows the third command, whatever its timestamp | A decision uses only packets whose arrival is not later than the decision; a packet is never visible before its arrival (§11.8) | §11.8 |

### N.9 §12 Lemma: proofs, certificates, the offline auditor

| CX | The tempting shortcut | The smallest case that breaks it | The rule it protects | Defined in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| CX-106 | A bundle whose hashes are all consistent is a valid certificate | A bundle with an unknown proof kind and consistent hashes: every digest matches and no checker implements the argument. The auditor returns `reject` | The registry of proof kinds is closed and versioned (ADR-LEMMA-04, INV-LEMMA-04, FROB-REF-823) | §12.4, §12.7 |
| CX-107 | A certificate that checks can be offered for any query about the same model | The docking cell (U, ε) = (¾, ¼) with the packet on time is accepted for the inclusion; the same bundle with its quantifiers reordered is rejected, although every hash is still consistent | The quantifier prefix, domain and assumption set of a certificate must match the query (FROB-REF-826) | §12.4, §12.7 |
| CX-108 | A witness is valid if its numbers are right, whatever order they are listed in | Stance specimen: forces (160, 130, 110, 0) N are typed to the port order [++, +−, −+, −−]. With the foot order swapped and the forces unchanged the total is still 400 N and the primitive moment equations no longer hold. Three further mutations must also be rejected: an unreported positive force floor; a passive-authority tag; (60, 30) passed as the torque | Every field that enters the argument is typed with frame, origin, sign and units, and the primitive equations travel in the bundle (§12.4, FROB-REF-829, GATE-LEMMA-03) | §12.4 |
| CX-109 | An exact argument yields a `Certified` result | An exact rational argument over an `Estimated` material card is `Estimated`; an energy budget computed exactly in simulation over `Estimated` inputs is `Estimated` | The class of a combined result is the minimum of its inputs' classes; nothing converts a claim product into a proof (INV-LEMMA-02, FROB-REF-834, GATE-LEMMA-06) | §12.2, C-31 |
| CX-110 | A study receipt in a bundle proves the study's conclusion | The auditor recomputes the arithmetic of a recorded study (counts, e-values, spent α); it cannot attest that the episodes ran | `statistical-receipt` is never a proof: class at most `Tested` (ADR-LEMMA-06) | §12.4, §12.5 |
| CX-111 | A reassociation is a harmless rewrite and can be filed as bit-exact (class E) | A rewrite trace that contains a reassociation filed under class E is replayed by the independent checker and rejected: reassociation is not in the closed table of class-E rules | A class-E trace uses only rules of the closed table (T-27); equality over ℝ is the theorem, and bit-exactness in floating point is a test | §12.7, §12.4 |
| CX-112 | A kernel certificate shows that the kernel's floating-point output satisfies the identity | Certificates are about the straight-line program over ℚ with square roots; rounding is not modeled, and round-off breaks exact equivariance | The floating-point gap is measured by residual tests with dimensioned tolerances and by the power-of-two test of C-21, not proved (§12.3, §12.5) | §12.3, §12.5 |
| CX-113 | The articulated variational step is symplectic, like the free-body step | False as stated for a configuration-dependent mass matrix: a kick–drift splitting is then no longer the variational step | No symplectic claim beyond one rigid body; energy behaviour is measured (§12.3, C-36, C-05) | §12.3 |
| CX-114 | The boundary value of a threshold can be reported as the attained optimum | Docking family (T-23): for U ∈ [½, 1] a robust causal policy exists if and only if the packet is on time and ε < (U + ½)/4. At U = ¾ the bound is 5/16 and ε = 5/16 is not admitted: the supremum is not attained. The stance minimum b = 3/14 m is attained and carries a matching lower bound (the dual witness for every smaller b) | A minimum is claimed only with the incumbent value and a checked matching lower bound; an infimum that is not attained is returned as an open threshold with a feasible candidate and its gap (§12.4) | §12.4, §12.3 |
| CX-115 | An artifact with no authorization fields is neutral | `frob-verify` rejects an artifact in which a flag is missing, exactly as it rejects one in which `execution_authorized` is true | Every exported artifact carries the three flags, false, and no code path sets one (INV-LEMMA-03, FROB-REF-833) | §12.2, §12.7 |

### N.10 §13 Marrow: the algebraic core

| CX | The tempting shortcut | The smallest case that breaks it | The rule it protects | Defined in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| CX-116 | A free-body integrator that conserves kinetic energy has the right coadjoint sign (equivalently: ad* is "the negative transpose" of ad) | Inertia diag(1, 2, 3) kg·m², ω = (1, 1, 0) rad/s, π = (1, 2, 0). Correct law: π̇ = π × ω = (0, 0, −1). Reversed sign: (0, 0, +1). Both give ω·π̇ = 0, so kinetic energy is conserved either way. The body-frame rate of spatial angular momentum π̇ + ω × π is (0, 0, 0) with the correct sign and (0, 0, 2) with the wrong one | ADR-MARROW-05 (two coadjoints, each with its own type and sign); the free-body and coadjoint-pair laws of §13.8; the mutation rule (each convention guarded by at least three fixtures); GATE-MARROW-04 | §13.3, §13.10 |
| CX-117 | The lower three rows of a space screw Jacobian are the point Jacobian of the tool | Planar 2R arm, links 0.4 m and 0.3 m, angles 0 and π/2, tool force (10, 0) N. The point Jacobian is [[−0.3, −0.3], [0.4, 0.0]] m/rad and gives joint torques (−3, −3) N·m; the lower rows of the space screw Jacobian give (0, 0) | Frame-typed Jacobians and wrench maps (`WrenchPushforward` against `WrenchPullback`, §13.3); the planar 2R known answer | §13.10 |
| CX-118 | A mechanically symmetric robot may tie policy weights, augment data or deduplicate designs under its mechanical group | A quadruped built fore-aft and left-right symmetric has 𝔾_mech = C₂ × C₂ of order 4. One forward lidar on the torso is fixed by the left-right mirror and moved by the fore-aft mirror and the half-turn, so 𝔾_obs = C₂ of order 2. A policy tied under C₂ × C₂ must walk backwards as the mirror image of walking forwards while seeing only forwards | ADR-MARROW-06; PO-MARROW-04; GATE-MARROW-03; FROB-REF-109 for a false declaration | §13.4 |
| CX-119 | A failed search over symmetric (tied, deterministic) policies shows the task is infeasible | Two identical latches, of which exactly one must be closed. At an observation fixed by the mirror a deterministic equivariant controller must issue an action fixed by the mirror, so it commands both latches alike, and both matching commands fail. A controller that draws one latch at random is equivariant in law and succeeds | A failed search excludes only the policy class searched; `frob-algebra` exposes fixed subspaces so that A-03 lists the observations at which tying forces tied actions | §13.4 |
| CX-120 | A task that is symmetric in law may be reduced by symmetry inside each world | A quadruped crossing a patch that is slippery on one side only. The world distribution can be mirror-invariant in law, which licenses augmentation; no single world is mirror-invariant, which forbids reduction inside it | Reduction inside one world uses Stab(context), not 𝔾_obs; the table of uses in §13.4 | §13.4 |
| CX-121 | If the encoder permutation of a symmetry still exists, the symmetry still holds | Changing one foot's sole material leaves the encoder permutation intact and breaks the transition symmetry: the representation exists and the group element does not | 𝔾_mech preserves contact laws and interface cards; both facts are recorded by A-03; GATE-MARROW-03 lists a changed sole material among its planted asymmetries | §13.4 |
| CX-122 | Every irreducible block of an equivariant layer takes one real scalar per pair of copies | C₃ permuting three legs cyclically. The equivariant maps ℝ³ → ℝ³ are the circulants, three parameters: the trivial block plus one two-dimensional block of complex type, 1 + 2 = 3. Giving the two-dimensional block one scalar yields two parameters and only the symmetric circulants, which are the D₃-equivariant maps; x ↦ (x₂ − x₃, x₃ − x₁, x₁ − x₂) becomes unrepresentable | ADR-MARROW-07 (real and complex blocks, Reynolds operator as oracle); PO-MARROW-05; the blocks law of §13.8 | §13.5 |
| CX-123 | A strict inequality that selects a discrete outcome can be evaluated in plain single precision | The single-precision numbers nearest 0.3, 0.4 and 0.5 in x² + y² ≤ z². In single precision the two sides are equal, so the plain test reports the point inside the cone. The exact value of x² + y² − z² for those stored numbers is +1.19 × 10⁻⁸: the point is outside. A fused multiply-add gives a third rounding sequence | ADR-MARROW-08; the `Guarded` class with its exact integer fallback; the `frob-kir` lint against `Plain` predicates on selects whose arms differ; GATE-MARROW-05 | §13.7 |
| CX-124 | A mirrored revolute joint can be written with the mirrored axis P·a and keep its coordinate | For a reflection (det P = −1) an axis is an axial vector and maps to det(P)·P·a. A design that writes P·a instead must negate the coordinate, its limits, and its encoder and gear signs; hiding the sign inside a rigid transform is how mirrored encoders read backwards | ADR-MARROW-09 (a reflection is an `Isometry` with an explicit determinant, not a `Transform`); the reflections law of §13.8; the planted mutation "a missing parity sign on a mirrored axis" | §13.2, §13.3 |

### N.11 §14 Skeleton: design language, modules, archetypes, preflight

| CX | The tempting shortcut | The smallest case that breaks it | The rule it protects | Defined in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| CX-125 | A body with no stated fill can default to solid | Torso box 0.42 × 0.20 × 0.10 m in an alloy at 2,700 kg/m³: solid it weighs 22.7 kg; as a 2.5 mm shell it weighs 1.92 kg. The solid reading is 11.8 times too heavy, and through the mass spiral (loop gain 0.59, 2.4 kg of robot per kilogram of structure) a 20.8 kg error becomes about 50 kg of robot, with every actuator sized for it | ADR-SKEL-03 (no default fill); INV-SKEL-05; FROB-REF-105; the solid-fill advisory above one quarter of `mass_max` | §14.5 |
| CX-126 | Rescaling the density to hit the right mass repairs the inertia | The same box about its vertical axis: radius of gyration squared (0.42² + 0.20²)/12 = 0.0180 m² as a solid and 0.0236 m² as a shell, 31% larger, because a shell keeps its mass at the walls | The fill rule (shell moments as the difference of two solids); PO-SKEL-02; GATE-SKEL-07 | §14.5 |
| CX-127 | A declared inertia that passes pseudo-inertia positivity is physically possible for its part | A 1 kg part with principal moments 0.008 kg·m² each, declared inside a sphere of radius 0.10 m: the mass-weighted mean squared distance tr(I_C)/(2m) is 0.012 m², above R² = 0.010 m². No body inside the sphere has that inertia; a thin shell of that radius meets the bound with equality | The mass-support bound ‖c − o‖² + tr(I_C)/(2m) ≤ R² and tr(P·Q) ≥ 0 at stage 2; FROB-REF-106 | §14.5, §13.10 ≡ CX-021 |
| CX-128 | A contract that carries peak demands can exclude a design (add the peaks and compare with the limit) | Two fingers share a bus limited to one unit of current, each with peak demand one unit. Adding peaks gives two units and prunes the design; one finger draws at the first tick and the other at the second, so the schedule is feasible | A `bus_demand` without a time-resolved profile is usable for admission only; the bus analysis of §15.10 runs in full before any electrical exclusion; GATE-SKEL-06 | §14.6 |
| CX-129 | A limb's port inertia is positive definite, so it can be inverted at the port and can stand for a limb in contact | Eliminating a joint with axis s gives I − I·s·(sᵀI·s)⁻¹·sᵀI, which sends s to zero; with I the identity and s the first basis vector the packet is diag(0, 1, 1, 1, 1, 1). A chain of massless parents is singular, and contact rows couple every limb through the base | The `SymPsd6` type of `LimbPortPacket`; PO-SKEL-04; the three consumer rules of §14.6; stage 2 refuses massless chains; KILL-SKEL-PORT | §14.6 |
| CX-130 | Swapping one leaf module leaves the robot's verdicts and rollouts valid | A leaf-local substitution reuses siblings' subtrees, kinematics kernels and local analyses, and still invalidates static stability, the baseline controller, every rollout, every verdict and every whole-robot certificate, because ground contact couples every limb through the base; an edit to the shared bus or sink is global | The four conditions of leaf-local substitution; INV-SKEL-06, INV-SKEL-09; GATE-SKEL-06 (the mirror swap reuses, the shared-bus edit invalidates, a reversed port sign refuses) | §14.6 |
| CX-131 | A connection-curvature (bracket) test decides whether a wheel layout can produce the required motions | A mecanum base with all four roller axes parallel: the twist-to-wheel map drops from rank 3 to rank 2, the base slides freely along one direction, and the growth vector is (2, 3) where an omnidirectional base must have (3). The curvature test passes, as it passes a differential drive, because the missing direction is still reached by a bracket | Stage 4 runs the growth vector on wheeled and tracked bases and curvature on shape-changers; FROB-REF-118 | §14.11 |
| CX-132 | A full-rank local information matrix means the sensors can tell the admitted worlds apart | Range beacons at (−1, 0) and (1, 0). A robot at (0, 1) and a robot at (0, −1) both read √2 from each, and the local information matrix is the identity at both points; a task that requires moving toward the positive side needs opposite actions in the two worlds. A third beacon at (0, 2) reads 1 and 3 and separates them when the range error is below 1 | Stage 8 alias search (`aliased-passive`, `aliased-all-probes`); FROB-REF-120; the bound (1 + TV)/2; KILL-SKEL-ALIAS | §14.11 |
| CX-133 | A symmetry the designer declares can be used by consumers | `quad-mammal` with a forward lidar and `assert_obs: C2xC2`: the computed 𝔾_obs is C₂, and the design is refused (FROB-REF-109) with the lidar mount as breaker | INV-SKEL-07 (a declared symmetry never replaces a computed one, and no consumer reads the declaration) | §14.14 |
| CX-134 | A wheel edit can be judged by one of its two effects at a threshold | At a 0.012 m lip, raising the wheel radius from 0.060 m to 0.075 m lengthens the lever arm √(2·r·h − h²) from 36.0 mm to 40.7 mm while the horizontal push over axle load falls from 0.750 to 0.646; the chapter states both figures and names "a wheel edit judged by one of its two effects" as what the row guards against | The raised-threshold oracle of the `diff-drive` archetype; the known answers of §14.14 | §14.10, §14.14 |

### N.12 §15 Sinew: actuators, transmissions, power

| CX | The tempting shortcut | The smallest case that breaks it | The rule it protects | Defined in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| CX-135 | One efficiency multiplier describes a gear in all four quadrants | η = 0.8, N = 10, the load driving the joint with 10 N·m at 1 rad/s: 10 W in. The single-multiplier law τ_joint = η·N·τ_motor gives τ_motor = 1.25 N·m at 10 rad/s, so 12.5 W arrives at the motor and 2.5 W came from nowhere. The four-quadrant law gives 0.8 N·m and 8 W, with 2 W lost | Four-quadrant loss with η_f and η_b; INV-SINEW-01; FROB-REF-130; GATE-SINEW-08 | §15.9 |
| CX-136 | Copper resistance can be held constant in the thermal check | Card with R₀ = 0.20 Ω, α = 0.00393 K⁻¹, G = 0.80 W/K, C = 120 J/K, ambient 25 °C, limit 155 °C, at a constant 20 A. Constant resistance: rise 80/0.80 = 100 K, 125 °C, inside the limit, which is never reached. With R(T): s = 0.314 W/K, rise 80/(0.80 − 0.314) = 164.7 K, 190 °C, outside the limit, reached at 385 s. The largest continuous current falls from 22.8 A to 18.6 A, and above I_crit = 31.9 A there is no equilibrium at all | ADR-SINEW-04 (the static check uses the corrected law); FROB-REF-132; GATE-SINEW-05 | §15.7 |
| CX-137 | Under torque holding the runaway threshold can be estimated from the slope of the loss curve at T₀, and the continuous rating leaves margin below it | Same card with α_B = −0.0011 K⁻¹. The slope estimate √(G/(R₀·(α − 2·α_B))) gives 25.5 A; the loss curve is convex and the equilibrium vanishes at a tangency at 16.5 A cold-equivalent. The limit is reached at 15.9 A, so the continuous rating and the runaway threshold are four percent apart | `actuator.thermal` solves the tangency and reports the margin to it; GATE-SINEW-05 (tangency within 1%) | §15.7 |
| CX-138 | Episodes that start at ambient temperature test the thermal limits of a design | In 20 s from cold at 20 A the winding of the card rises by 164.7·(1 − e^(−20/247)) = 12.8 K, against an equilibrium rise of 164.7 K. A policy trained and evaluated on such episodes never meets derating | ADR-SINEW-08: thermal state is initialized from a declared duty history (`cold`, `soaked(T)`, `steady(task)`); evaluation defaults to `steady(task)` | §15.7 |
| CX-139 | A torque-speed envelope without the voltage ellipse is good enough above base speed | Card with λ = 0.010 V·s, L = 100 µH, I_max = 40 A, V_bus = 48 V: base speed 3,151 rad/s, no-load speed 3,394 rad/s. At 1.5 times base speed the motor delivers 59% of peak torque with field weakening (i_d = −32.2 A, i_q = 23.7 A) and none without it, because that speed is above the no-load speed. A model without the ellipse errs in one direction or the other | The dq voltage limit at L2; `actuator.envelope`; GATE-SINEW-06; the L1 flag when L_q·I_max exceeds one tenth of the flux linkage | §15.8 |
| CX-140 | Reflected rotor inertia is diagonal in joint space | Symmetric differential with q₁ = (θ₁ + θ₂)/2 and q₂ = (θ₁ − θ₂)/2: the reflected inertia T⁻ᵀ·diag(J_r)·T⁻¹ is [[J₁ + J₂, J₁ − J₂], [J₁ − J₂, J₁ + J₂]]. Unequal rotors couple the two joints | Coupling transmissions as a matrix transformer; the known answer of §15.9 | §15.9 |
| CX-141 | A bus under load can always deliver the commanded power at a lower voltage (or: bus feasibility is a current limit) | Actuators under current control draw power. A bus with E = 48 V and R = 0.08 Ω can deliver at most E²/(4·R) = 7,200 W; at 1,800 W it sits at 44.8 V and 40.2 A. A demand above the ceiling has no operating point: the voltage collapses and the step records a brownout event; it never completes at the requested power | FROB-REF-133 at stage 6; the brownout event; GATE-SINEW-07 (100 of 100 trials, never a completed step); INV-SINEW-04 and FROB-REF-135 for exhaustion, which no kernel clips | §15.10 |
| CX-142 | Losses that flow into thermal nodes are also listed as energy that left the system | Copper, iron, friction and gear losses are stored in thermal nodes; the energy that leaves is mechanical work at the joint and heat to ambient. Counting stored heat again as a loss produces a false defect in the balance | The identity of §15.11 (decrease of chemical energy = work out + increase of stored energies + heat to ambient + residual); INV-SINEW-02; GATE-SINEW-02 | §15.11 |
| CX-143 | Agreement between the referee and the fast tiers is evidence that the actuator card is right | Every tier reads the same card. If the card is wrong, the referee, the CPU batch tier and both GPU tiers are wrong together, and their agreement says nothing about it | Randomization over the card's stated uncertainty; the `card-conditional` label of A-16; refusal of verdicts on `assumed` fields under `--strict-data`; measurement in §30 as the only evidence about the card | §15.12 |

### N.13 §16 Kinesis: dynamics on three tiers

| CX | The tempting shortcut | The smallest case that breaks it | The rule it protects | Defined in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| CX-144 | A rejected step may redraw its random inputs | A fair sign as the input; a step is rejected whenever the sign is negative and then redrawn once. The accepted sign is positive with probability ½ + ½·½ = ¾: rejection that redraws biases the law | INV-KIN-03 (a random input is a function of its address and interval and is never redrawn); the dyadic refinement law (PO-KIN-04); FROB-REF-223; GATE-KIN-04 | §16.5 |
| CX-145 | Simultaneous events can be solved one at a time, with constraints imposed afterwards | Two 1 kg sliders joined by a rigid tie reach a wall together at 1 m/s. Stopping both needs a wall impulse of 2 N·s. Solving the wall contact for the first slider alone gives 1 N·s; imposing the tie afterwards leaves 1 kg·m/s shared by 2 kg, so both move into the wall at 0.5 m/s | Coupled impulses per island with W = J·P·Jᵀ (step T4.6); simultaneous touchdowns are ordinary inputs; GATE-KIN-04 (exact to 10⁻¹² N·s) | §16.5 |
| CX-146 | A reduction over a padded axis may group its terms by the buffer's capacity | In `f32` with a = 10⁸, b = −10⁸, c = 1: (a + b) + c = 1 and a + (b + c) = 0, because the spacing near 10⁸ is 8. A grouping that depended on capacity would make a world's bits depend on its neighbours | PO-KIN-03 (pairwise trees aligned to powers of two, real entries in a prefix, canonical zero +0); INV-KIN-04; GATE-KIN-05; GATE-PHYLUM-02 | §16.6 |
| CX-147 | Penetration can be removed by a position-level correction at no cost in energy | Removing a depth d in one step gives the body a velocity d/h from nothing. With m = 1 kg, d = 1 mm and h = 2 ms that is 0.5 m/s and 0.125 J per event. The compliant law on the same penetration at k = 10⁵ N/m stores 0.05 J and returns less. A hopping policy that finds the first behaviour gains height from the solver | INV-KIN-06; the `energy-suspect` label (created energy above 1% of actuator work per episode); the first planted exploit of GATE-FID-06 | §16.10 |
| CX-148 | A four-sided friction pyramid with the same μ is equivalent to the cone for ranking designs | A pyramid grants √2·μ along its diagonals, 41% more than the cone, so a preference can exploit diagonal traction; the cone gives μ·N in every direction | The model-form rank check (cone arm) returning `ModelConditional`; the second planted exploit of GATE-FID-05 and GATE-FID-06; GATE-TRAC-04 | §16.9, §16.10, §18.16 |
| CX-149 | A state error inside the fidelity envelope means the two tiers agree | The fidelity ledger also records task decisions: success, terminal class and the verdict of a comparison. A state error inside its envelope with a different decision is a disagreement (no numeric case is given in the chapter) | Decision-level equivalence (§35) as a ledger quantity; the promotion rule | §16.8 |
| CX-150 | A world whose solver gave up can be scored as a failed episode | A world that exhausts its retry ladder ends `Faulted` with cause `numerical-fault` (FROB-REF-221) and publishes nothing from the failed interval; it is kept apart from `fall`, `slip` and `collision`, and summaries state how many worlds were excluded for this cause (no numeric case is given in the chapter) | ADR-KIN-05; the failure taxonomy of §29; step T7 of the transaction | §16.5 |
| CX-151 | The referee can adjudicate every scenario the fast tiers run | FrankenSim's solve admits at most 21 contacts. A sole with eight samples per foot fits when standing (16) and overflows in a fall or a crawl; six feet down with four samples each need 24; a tracked robot with patch samples along two belts overflows in normal driving | FROB-REF-721 (a named capacity refusal, never a truncated contact set); the referee's own coverage reported per class (GATE-FID-03); `established` names its adjudicator where coverage is below 80% | §16.4, §16.6, §16.11 |
| CX-152 | A sampled CPU referee shadows most of a GPU run | A humanoid batch at 0.5 M world-steps/s and h = 2 ms produces 1,000 world-seconds per second and a quadruped batch at 2 M produces 4,000; a referee at 0.2 to 1.0 times real time on 24 cores produces 4.8 to 24. The share is 0.5 to 2.4% and 0.1 to 0.6%. A five-point gap in success rate needs 576 to 1,600 episodes per tier, 8 minutes to 1.9 hours of background referee time | The `referee_coverage` figure on every receipt; GATE-FID-07 (at least 0.1%, reported); the background referee is a correctness guard and removes no training | §16.11 |

### N.14 §17 Phylum: families of bodies in one batch

| CX | The tempting shortcut | The smallest case that breaks it | The rule it protects | Defined in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| CX-153 | A design sweep may be quotiented under the robot's mechanical symmetry group | Ten leg types on the six slots of a radial hexapod: 86,185 orbits under the dihedral group of order 12, a reduction of 11.6. With a front-only sensor only the sagittal mirror preserves observations, and the count is (10⁶ + 10³)/2 = 500,500, a reduction of 2.0. Quotienting under the larger group merges designs the closed loop can tell apart | INV-PHYLUM-03 (quotients use Γ_obs only); FROB-REF-161; GATE-PHYLUM-01 (the lidar control) | §17.11, §17.18 |
| CX-154 | Placements with the same first moment are the same experiment | Ballast fixture: eight identical slugs in 24 collinear slots enter rigid-body dynamics through the count, Σx and Σx². Of C(24, 8) = 735,471 placements there are 24,546 distinct (Σx, Σx²) pairs. Two placements with equal Σx and different Σx² must fall in different classes (slots 1 and 4 against slots 2 and 3, beside six common slugs, give Σx = 5 twice and Σx² = 17 against 13) | The mission-observable digest hashes exactly what the step plan and the task read; GATE-PHYLUM-01 (the equal-Σx control); KILL-QUOTIENT | §17.11, §17.18 |
| CX-155 | A local summary that carries peak demand is a sound key for pruning or reuse | Two fingers with identical local summaries on a bus limited to one unit of current have no feasible one-tick schedule and two feasible two-tick schedules. A contract that adds peak demands prunes a feasible design; a local summary without time correlation is not a sound key | INV-PHYLUM-04; the negative controls of the invalidation contract; GATE-PHYLUM-05 | §17.11 |
| CX-156 | Members of a symmetry orbit give equal outcomes at equal world addresses | The design g·d in world w behaves as d does in world g⁻¹·w: orbit members have equal outcome distributions, not equal outcomes at equal addresses. Distributional results are shared; a per-address result is shared only after the address is mapped through the group action, and a comparison paired by address against a third design is re-run | The orbit rule of §17.11; the negative control "an unmapped per-address result reused" | §17.11 |
| CX-157 | Equal mission digests prove that two designs give identical traces | The statement is about the simulator and cannot be discharged in a proof kernel. It holds by construction, because the kernels read nothing but the tables, and is checked by replay of sampled pairs inside a class (class Tested, D2) | "Structural, not proved"; GATE-PHYLUM-01 (zero false quotients in 10⁴ sampled class pairs); KILL-QUOTIENT | §17.11 |
| CX-158 | A memo key can be written by hand from the inputs the author remembers | A dependency omitted from a key returned a stale promotion in an executed test | Keys are derived from declared inputs; one hit in 64 is recomputed in shadow and one mismatch stops serving that node kind; FROB-REF-162; INV-PHYLUM-04 | §17.11 |
| CX-159 | Exact reuse after an edit is a speed lever | An edit that touches a shared battery bus, a shared thermal sink, a kinematic loop across branches or any mass or geometry of a robot in contact invalidates every episode-level result of the design, because the floating body couples all of it; exact reuse after a dynamics-affecting edit is at most about a tenth of compute (FACT, Appendix H) | The invalidation contract is hygiene and an evidence rule; INV-PHYLUM-04 | §17.11 |
| CX-160 | Padding a mixed batch to its largest robot costs about the mean-to-maximum ratio | 89% at 18 degrees of freedom, 10% at 35 and 1% at 66: r = 0.31, and the overhead is 3.27 on linear stages, 9.59 on quadratic ones and 23.3 on cubic ones. One large hybrid in a hundred designs makes a single padded batch cost three to twenty-three times the unpadded work | 1/r ≤ Ω_p ≤ r⁻ᵖ (PO-PHYLUM-02); size buckets with a cap of eight; FROB-REF-741 | §17.5 |
| CX-161 | Finer buckets always pay | Worked card (90% at 18, 10% at 35, τ = 0.5 µs, launch overhead 0.45 ms per plan): at 8,192 worlds two buckets cost 5.0 ms against 14.8 ms; at 512 worlds 1.16 ms against 1.35 ms; at 128 worlds the single padded bucket wins, 0.67 ms against 0.96 ms. The crossover is N = c_L/(τ·(Ω − Ω_b)) = 360 worlds | The bucket rule N·τ·(Ω − Ω_b) > (B − 1)·c_L; the planner's dynamic program; `phylum.plan` | §17.5 |
| CX-162 | Mixing many designs in one batch is a large speedup at any batch shape | Against D = 512 designs run one after another with W worlds each, at Ω = 1.3: about 40 at W = 16, 11 at W = 64, 3.5 at W = 256, 1.4 at W = 1,024 and 1.1 at W = 2,048. The benefit is occupancy at few worlds per design and vanishes near a thousand worlds per design; a comparator that batches parameter variants, compiles in parallel or keeps one template per family closes most of the gap | The ceiling stated before any speed test; the four arms of the head-to-head (§17.14); GATE-PHYLUM-03; KILL-PHYLUM | §17.5 |
| CX-163 | A pruning ratio and a kernel speedup multiply; improving most of the work gives a large factor | speedup ≤ 1/(1 − f + f/r + h). Improving 85% of the work without limit cannot reach ten (the bound is 6.7); with f = 0.97, r = 40 and h = 0.02 the bound is about 13.5; a pruning ratio is never multiplied by a speedup measured on work the pruning already removed. For the family lane f = 0.15 to 0.35 and r at most 4 give 1.10 to 1.32 | The speedup bound of §17.6 with "matched" work on the strongest baseline's critical path; KILL-FAMILYLANE | §17.6 |
| CX-164 | A kernel specialized to one design gives a large step speedup | In a published humanoid step trace everything a design-specialized recursion could touch sums to 53 of 262 ns, 20% of the step. An infinitely fast specialized recursion gives 1.25 and one twice as fast gives 1.11; on a manipulation scene the share is 16% and the ceiling 1.19 | Specialization is funded only if the M2 profile shows a specializable share of at least one third; KILL-SPECIALIZE (gain below 1.2 or compile above 60 s) | §17.9 |
| CX-165 | A finite-difference design gradient can be taken of any metric over any horizon | A binary outcome has zero gradient almost everywhere and a finite-difference variance that grows as 1/δ. In contact-rich simulation a perturbation of 5 × 10⁻⁴ degree has been reported to diverge after 50 steps; after the active contact sets of a pair differ, its difference is noise of variance about σ²/(2δ²) | FROB-REF-540; the horizon cap of 50 control intervals; truncation at the divergence step with the `truncated-horizon` label; INV-PHYLUM-07; KILL-FDGRAD | §17.13 |
| CX-166 | Splitting designs into training and held-out sets by seed keeps the test set unseen | A mirrored copy of a training design is the same closed-loop system; split by seed alone it can appear in the test set (no numeric case is given in the chapter) | `DesignSplit` keeps every orbit of the observation-preserving group on one side; GATE-PHYLUM-07 (no orbit straddles a split) | §17.10 |

### N.15 §18 Traction: collision, contact, wheels, tracks, terrain

| CX | The tempting shortcut | The smallest case that breaks it | The rule it protects | Defined in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| CX-167 | A positive nominal gap is clearance | A nominal gap of 6 mm with 4 mm of outward error on each side leaves the outer enclosures overlapping by 2 mm: the answer is `unknown` until an error term is reduced. With 10 mm nominal and the same errors the answer is `clear` with 2 mm to spare | INV-TRAC-03 (`clear` from disjoint outer enclosures only, `penetrates` from intersecting inner enclosures only); GATE-TRAC-07; strict inequalities by outward rounding or exact-integer kernels | §18.3, §18.16 |
| CX-168 | Two swept volumes that overlap in space collide | The bodies can occupy the shared region at different times and never touch; the test is per time slab, never on whole-sweep volumes (no numeric case is given in the chapter) | The per-slab enclosure test of §18.3; GATE-TRAC-07 | §18.3 |
| CX-169 | If the gap is positive at both ends of a step, no contact occurred in it | A sample sphere of radius 2 mm moves at 5 m/s with h = 2 ms, so it travels 10 mm. A 2 mm plate lies 1 mm ahead. Clearing the plate takes 1 + 4 + 2 = 7 mm, so at the end of the step the sphere is 3 mm beyond the far face: both endpoints are clear and the step encloses a collision | Swept bounds and event location under transactional profiles; the speculative margin of at least v·h + ε_g on lockstep tiers; GATE-TRAC-06 (the endpoint-only mutation must miss); GATE-TRAC-03 | §18.4 |
| CX-170 | A scene distance field at working resolution represents all the geometry | A trilinear field on a 2 cm grid cannot hold a 2 mm plate: the plate vanishes or is thickened to the grid | Features thinner than two fine voxels are routed to thin primitives (`K-np-thin`) or the mesh path; FROB-REF-320 for a thin feature with no route | §18.3 |
| CX-171 | Sliding friction and torsional friction can be independent rows | With sliding speed v and spin ω on the four-dimensional cone the tangential force is μ·N·v/√(v² + (r_eff·ω)²), which is μ·N/√2 at v = r_eff·ω. Independent rows grant μ·N of sliding friction and μ·N·r_eff of torsional friction at once, 41% more in magnitude than the surface can supply; a foot that pivots while it slides would hold where it should slip | The four-dimensional cone (PO-TRAC-01); GATE-TRAC-04 (the independent-row mutation must fail) | §18.6 |
| CX-172 | Backlash can be modeled as a dead zone on the torque command | A dead zone produces no impact when the gap closes and no hunting in a position loop. The rows give both: a rotor of inertia 10⁻⁴ kg·m² at 5 rad/s behind a ratio of 10 crosses a 0.5° gap in β·N/ω₀ = 17.5 ms and, closing inelastically on an output of 0.05 kg·m², leaves it at J·N·ω₀/(J·N² + I_out) = 0.083 rad/s | Backlash as two unilateral rows on the relative angle with the rotor as its own coordinate; FROB-REF-241; GATE-TRAC-04; GATE-SINEW-04 | §18.7 |
| CX-173 | A tracked or skid-steer base can be modeled with rolling (no lateral slip) constraints | Exact lateral no-slip at contact stations a ahead of and behind the centre requires v_y + a·ω = 0 and v_y − a·ω = 0, hence ω = 0. The base turns only by slipping, so its turning is a property of the friction law | The belt law with an anisotropic friction ellipse and yaw scrub; the rejection fixture "turning in place at zero scrub work" (§14.13); the nonholonomic model of C-07 is a planning model and an oracle for rolling only | §18.9 |
| CX-174 | Diagonal scaling makes the Cholesky factor more accurate | Without pivoting, the computed factor of S·H·S for power-of-two S is S times the computed factor of H bit for bit, absent overflow and underflow: its rounding errors are already those of the scaled problem. What scaling repairs is everything around the factor: tolerances, stopping tests, line-search thresholds, iterative passes | PO-TRAC-03; GATE-TRAC-10; ADR-TRAC-07 | §18.12 |
| CX-175 | One tolerance on the unscaled Newton gradient serves every robot | Degrees of freedom mix metres and radians, and their diagonal entries differ by orders of magnitude, so one tolerance on the unscaled gradient is a dimension error; in scaled variables the gradient norm is dimensionless (no numeric case is given in the chapter) | Diagonal scaling in stage 8 (`K-scale-diag`); INV-TRAC-05 (impulses and forces typed apart; no tolerance applies to both) | §18.12, §16.5 |
| CX-176 | A half-precision factor with single-precision refinement cheapens the contact solve | Refinement contracts only if κ·u₁₆ < 1, with u₁₆ ≈ 4.9 × 10⁻⁴. A rank-one contact operator with proximal shift ρ = 10⁻²·tr(W)/48 for 16 contacts has κ = 1 + 48/0.01 = 4,801 and κ·u₁₆ = 2.34. Cost: k refinement steps cost 9k/n times the factor they replace: 2.3 at n = 27 with seven steps, 1.5 at n = 35 with six, 3.0 at n = 18 with six. Emulated on three reference models (FACT, Appendix H): κ·u₁₆ ≥ 1 in 37 to 64% of sampled contact states and failure in 9 to 18% | ADR-TRAC-07; FROB-REF-245; single precision admitted only below the scaled κ·u bound (FROB-REF-244) | §18.12 |
| CX-177 | A wet floor is a map of constant friction coefficients | By the integrated Stefan law, a film thins to 10 µm in 0.038 s under a 2 cm patch carrying 100 N on water, in 2.3 s under the same patch on cooking oil, and in 0.76 s under a 6 cm patch carrying 400 N on water. Against a stance of 0.3 s a small foot on water reaches boundary friction almost at once, while oil and large flat feet ride a film for the whole stance | The film law with per-contact film thickness in contact memory; GATE-TRAC-05; KILL-FILM (the law must change at least 10% of outcomes against a constant patch at μ_b) | §18.8 |
| CX-178 | A foot that has squeezed the film out stands on a dry floor | A squeezed film recovers to the boundary-lubricated coefficient μ_b, not to the dry coefficient μ_d; only h₀ = 0 returns μ_d exactly | INV-TRAC-07 | §18.8 |
| CX-179 | A wider sole gives more traction on a patchy floor | Sole straddling a low-grip strip (μ 0.25 inside, 0.8 outside, uniform pressure) with the boundary at offset z: the high-grip share is clip(½ + z/w, 0, 1). At z = 0 the capacity is 0.525·N for any width; at z = 12 mm it is 0.690·N for w = 40 mm and 0.619·N for w = 70 mm. Area alone does not predict traction | The sole-strip row of the known-answer battery; load-weighted quadrature of patches | §18.16 |
| CX-180 | When a world exceeds its contact capacity the extra contacts can be dropped | A comparison is honest only if both arms were simulated. A tracked design against a point-foot design under an artificially low cap must return `capacity-biased` and no winner; the label applies whenever the arms differ by more than one percentage point of worlds in `Degraded` or capacity-refused episodes | INV-TRAC-04; INV-KIN-05; FROB-REF-721, FROB-REF-722, FROB-REF-822; GATE-TRAC-06 | §18.11 |
| CX-181 | The convex (associated) friction model is Coulomb friction | Dropping the term μ·‖u_T‖ from the cone complementarity gives the associated model, in which a sliding contact acquires a separation velocity μ·‖u_T‖. CL-1 has that artifact in compliant form | CL-H as the written specification of the referee; the fidelity ledger measures the artifact; the contact-law arm of the model-form check; GATE-TRAC-09 (no separation velocity on a sliding block) | §18.5 |
| CX-182 | A library integrator for a differential drive can serve as the rolling oracle | Heading 32 rad, wheel speeds 1 and 1 + 6·10⁻¹³ m/s, track 0.5 m, 400 steps of 1/400 s: `fs-mbd::planar_drive` at the pinned commit is reported to return zero translation where about (0.8342, 0.5514) m is correct (FACT to recheck) | The closed form Δp = v·Δt·sinc(δ/2)·(cos(θ + δ/2), sin(θ + δ/2)) with sinc(0) = 1 is the oracle; the admission test at INT-FSIM-01 and the upstream ask | §18.9, §14.10 ≡ CX-029 |
| CX-183 | Friction memory can stay on a contact when its feature changes | A wheel rolling over a sharp sill moves its witnesses from face to edge to face. Tangential memory kept on the changed feature produces a couple the contact forces do not explain; the fixture compares work, impulse and the feature sequence under step refinement (bounds: 1% at h/4 and 10⁻⁹ N·m in `f64`) | INV-TRAC-06 (memory is transported or reset at every witness change, each reset an event); GATE-TRAC-06 | §18.4 |

### N.16 §19 Habitat: scenes, living worlds, clearance, retrofits

| CX | The tempting shortcut | The smallest case that breaks it | The rule it protects | Defined in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| CX-184 | Three labelled landmarks and a known length establish the registration of an imported scene | Landmarks A = (0, 0, 0), B = (1, 0, 0), C = (0, 1, 0). The reflection (x, y, z) ↦ (x, y, −z) fixes all three, so a mirrored room registers with zero residual. A fourth landmark D = (0, 0, 1) maps to (0, 0, −1) and shows a residual of 2 m | Four non-coplanar correspondences, LᵀL = c²·I and det L > 0 for any import that admits a parity change or an affine map; `FROB-REF-302`; PO-HAB-07; GATE-HAB-04 | §19.5.1 |
| CX-185 | A gravity witness can stand in for the fourth landmark | If the three landmarks lie in a vertical plane, the reflection through that plane fixes the landmarks and preserves gravity, so the mirrored room still registers | The fourth point must be non-coplanar, with a tetrahedron volume of at least 1% of the cube of the largest separation (TARGET); `FROB-REF-302` | §19.5.1 |
| CX-186 | A clearance verdict can be read from nominal surfaces | A nominal 6 mm gap at a doorway with 4 mm of possible outward error on each side: the outer enclosures overlap by 2 mm, so clearance is not established; the inner enclosures are 14 mm apart, so penetration is not witnessed. The nominal test says "clear"; the verdict is `unknown` | Clearance is established only by disjoint outer enclosures and penetration only by intersecting inner enclosures; anything else is `unknown` (§19.5.2, INV-HAB-06); GATE-HAB-04 | §19.5.2 |
| CX-187 | Overlapping swept volumes show a collision, and one penetrating world shows that a body must hit | The spatial sweeps of two bodies can overlap although the bodies are never in the same place at the same time; a universal penetration claim must cover every admitted world, not one (stated as two tested traps; no numeric case is given in this section) | Inner enclosures must intersect at the same time and pose in one jointly admissible world | §19.5.2 |
| CX-188 | Quantizing the distance field to 16 bits over the whole scene range is fine | Sixteen bits over ±10 m resolve 20 m / 65,536 ≈ 0.31 mm, which at a contact stiffness of 10⁵ N/m is a force step of about 30 N. Over ±8 cm the step is about 2.4 µm and the ripple about 0.12 N per contact sample | Stored values are clamp(φ, −T, +T) with T = 4 fine voxels; quantization ripple at most 1% of weight (GATE-HAB-01) | §19.4, §35.7 |
| CX-189 | A wet floor is a patch with a constant low friction coefficient | Time to thin a film to 10 µm, t = 3π·η·R⁴ / (4·F·h₁²): 0.038 s for a 20 mm foot at 100 N on water, 2.3 s for the same foot on cooking oil, 0.76 s for a 60 mm foot at 400 N on water. Against a stance of about 0.3 s the first reaches boundary contact and the other two do not, on the same patch | Film friction depends on dwell time, load and pad size (the film-friction law, GATE-HAB-06) | §19.10.2 |
| CX-190 | A fixed step is a sufficient proxy for a rug | Pushing an undriven wheel over an edge takes W·tan α with tan α = √(h·(2r − h)) / (r − h). A castor with r = 25 mm at a 10 mm edge needs 4/3 of its load, 67 N for a 50 N castor; a 2 kg rug resists sliding with about 0.5 × 19.6 N ≈ 10 N. The rug slides and folds long before the castor climbs; a fixed step would be climbed or would block | A rug is a thin free rigid slab with its own friction and an edge step (rigid proxies, GATE-HAB-08) | §19.10.2 |
| CX-191 | Film transport under a contact can be written with the contact indicator outside the divergence, 1·∇·(h·v̄) | Three cells with h = (0, 1, 0), the indicator on the middle cell only and v̄ = +1: the masked update removes Δt/Δx from the middle cell and adds it nowhere. The face-flux form moves the same amount into the right-hand cell | Conservative face-flux update; INV-HAB-07, PO-HAB-05; the mass gate of GATE-HAB-07 | §19.10.3 |
| CX-192 | Living matter means a resolved fluid, cloth or cable solver in every training world | A D3Q19 lattice needs 152 bytes per cell: a 10 m × 10 m × 3 m room at 1 cm is 45.6 GB for one world and about 374 TB at 8,192 worlds; a film 0.1–1 mm thick needs 39 GB per square metre; at a 50 Hz step with u = 1 m/s the lattice Mach number is 3.5 at 1 cm against a limit of 0.3; τ − ½ = 6 × 10⁻⁶ for water at 10 cm and 20 ms; with coupled matter in 5% of worlds a coupled world may cost at most 181 rigid worlds | ADR-HAB-07: three tiers, only the Solid tier on the critical path; KILL-LIVING | §19.10.1 |
| CX-193 | A scalar disc test decides whether a body fits | A rectangular base 0.60 m wide in a 0.90 m corridor with a right-angle turn. The inscribed disc (diameter 0.60 m) says every length fits; the circumscribed disc says a 1.30 m body (diagonal 1.43 m) cannot. By L ≤ 2√2·a − 2b = 1.346 m, the 1.30 m body passes and a 1.40 m body does not | Clearance complexes on SE(2) with a witness, a cut or `unresolved` (ADR-HAB-08); GATE-HAB-10 | §19.11 |
| CX-194 | The default clearance lattice alone answers doorway fits | At Δ = 5 cm and 32 headings with ρ = 0.40 m the cell error is 74.6 mm, so a doorway fit with total margin under 2e ≈ 149 mm is `unresolved`; with doors drawn uniformly from 0.70–0.90 m and bases from 0.45–0.70 m, about 22% of pairs fall there, and they are the near fits | Local refinement is part of the mechanism (e = 18.7 mm at 12.5 mm and 128 headings, 6.0 mm at 5 mm and 512); KILL-FCLEAR at more than 30% unresolved after refinement | §19.11 |
| CX-195 | A static rejection settles whether a wheel can climb | A wheel of r = 50 mm at a 20 mm edge needs a friction coefficient of 4/3, statically impossible; yet a body arriving at √(2·g·h) = 0.63 m/s has the energy to rise 20 mm before losses. In an executed planar sill study, 62 of 156 cases that a sampled static test rejected succeeded dynamically | Static rejection and static admission are candidates for a dynamic test, not substitutes; the retrofit search reruns the full procedure (§19.12 step 3) | §19.12, §19.11 |
| CX-196 | Splitting train and test scenes by `SceneId` prevents leakage | A layout and its mirror image have different `SceneId`s and the same aisles; a policy trained on one and scored on the other has seen the test | Splits are made over groups (base scene, mirrors, clutter and retrofit variants, shared brick pools, scans of one building); INV-HAB-09, `FROB-REF-311`; GATE-HAB-14 | §19.14 |
| CX-197 | Each world can have its own scene | 8,192 worlds each with its own 2 cm home is 8,192 × 75 MB = 614 GB, 26 times a 24 GB card and more than twice the working set of the large-memory machine; per-world state is only about 20–53 kB | ADR-HAB-05: one scene serves many worlds through a shared brick pool | §19.15 |
| CX-198 | A narrow band saves an order of magnitude of scene memory, and a 2 cm warehouse is 600 MB | A 60 m × 40 m × 8 m warehouse at 2 cm is 4.8 GB dense; 600 MB belongs to 4 cm. The band keeps a fraction min(1, (A/V)·t·s): at 2 cm it saves a factor of one to five, at 1 cm 1.5 to 11, and a cluttered home gains least | The memory table of §19.15; measured occupancy at GATE-HAB-13 | §19.15 |
| CX-199 | Gloss and friction can share one material property | A glossy tile can give a bad depth sample and low traction for unrelated reasons; the four-state tile (matte dry, glossy dry, matte wet, glossy wet) separates them: an optical change must leave physics digests unchanged and a contact change must leave ray outputs unchanged (no numeric case is given) | ADR-HAB-03: two maps joined only by declared latents; GATE-HAB-05; control: a build that derives friction from `r0` | §19.2, §19.5.4 |

### N.17 §20 Sensorium: sensors, clocks, observability

| CX | The tempting shortcut | The smallest case that breaks it | The rule it protects | Defined in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| CX-200 | Run a 30 Hz policy every thirteenth step of 400 Hz physics (sub-schedules as integer ratios of the physics tick) | 400/13 = 30.77 Hz. Over a 20 s episode that is 615 decisions instead of 600, and a reward counted per decision is overpaid by 2.6%. The exact schedule (Q = 1,200) realizes decisions at boundaries 0, 14, 27, 40 with lateness 0, 2, 1, 0 quanta and a long-run rate of exactly 30 Hz | ADR-SENS-02: rational periods with integer phase accumulators; INV-SENS-04; GATE-SENS-04 | §20.4.6 |
| CX-201 | An exposure window may be closed at both ends | An impact of 0.2 N·s in one 10 ms exposure of a force sensor contributes 20 N to that window's average and nothing to the adjacent one; a closed-closed window counts an impulse on the shared instant in both | One half-open window convention [open, close); an impulse exactly at t belongs to the window that opens at t (§20.4.3); GATE-SENS-04 | §20.4.3, §20.20 |
| CX-202 | A camera exposure that straddles a step can blend the two end poses linearly | Across a boundary at which an impact reset a velocity, a linear blend fabricates motion blur and an inertial acceleration that never occurred (no numeric case is given) | Interpolation never crosses an impact boundary; the camera samples the segmented pose history (§20.4.4); GATE-SENS-06 | §20.4.4 |
| CX-203 | Sensor dropout is an independent coin per sample, so repeated reads average it away | A sensor reads a payload class correctly three times in four. With independent errors a majority of three reads is right 27/32 of the time, of five 459/512, of seven 3,807/4,096 (93%). With an error common to all reads (the same pane of glass, the same glare) the answer stays right three times in four however many reads are taken | ADR-SENS-08: dropout is a function of geometry, the optical card and one latent per region and episode; GATE-SENS-07 | §20.6 ≡ CX-052 |
| CX-204 | An active sensor in steam is attenuated once | The two-way power fraction is exp(−2σ·ℓ): with σ = 2 m⁻¹ and a 5% threshold every return is lost behind more than ln(20)/4 = 0.75 m; the one-way law exp(−σ·ℓ) keeps returns the two-way law loses | Optical volumes attenuate an active sensor over the round trip (§20.6); GATE-SENS-07 | §20.6, §20.20 |
| CX-205 | A full-rank observability Gramian means the robot knows where it is | Two aisles that are exact copies of each other give a full-rank local Gramian in each, yet nothing distinguishes one aisle from the other. In the corridor case W = (N/s²)·diag(0, 1) already has the null vector (1, 0), and one end wall restores rank; rank is a local statement | The Gramian covers infinitesimal ambiguity; finite ambiguity needs the alias witness (§20.12); GATE-SENS-08, GATE-SENS-09 | §20.11 |
| CX-206 | More training on the present sensors can satisfy any mission that is solvable world by world | T-junction: a charging plate at the end of the left branch in world L and of the right branch in world R, branches 10 m, a planar lidar that cannot see the plate beyond 0.5 m, a battery good for 12 m. Each world alone is solvable; the transcripts are identical up to the turn; a wrong turn costs 9.5 + 9.5 + 10 = 29 m.
